# A02 — AUDIT: Configuration and effective settings

**Status:** DRAFT — the AUDIT of topic A02 (Document 04, section 4, step 6), for ChatGPT's review
**Date:** 2026-11-08
**Revision history:** First version, 2026-11-08 (fieldwork from 06-Nov-2026). Corrected, 2026-11-08, after ChatGPT's review of the same day: F-19 withdrawn and F-18 recorded as information, not counted (Rama's instruction of 08-Nov-2026, relayed in ChatGPT's review of the same day); V-12 reclassified MATCH and V-14 UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED under the rule of section 1.6 on unestablished intent; X-01's basis stated; the counts, the handoffs and the findings for Rama recalculated; a focused independent check of the corrected records (section 14), after which F-15 was reclassified UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, the bases of V-08 and L-15 were stated in their results, and five handoff and wording points were corrected (two working commits). Clarified, 2026-11-08, after ChatGPT's second review of the same day and its decisions on the proposal: F-22 set out as three subtopics, of which the daily loss limit (0.03) and `gap_fade_long.min_score` (0) are answered by the later-dated sources and only the revert procedure is put to Rama (Open Issues Register A02-01); V-08's basis bounded to what the code shows, its sources kept apart, and the delivery-counterpart intent added to A02-08; T-01's condition (a) stated for each sub-case; F-22's KNOWN DEFECT reasoning restated against the four conditions with the revised evidence (condition (b) fails; F-22 stays ISSUE); the two KNOWN DEFECTs provisional until the review and the issue discussion; part L's wording on C10c aligned with section 4.
**Purpose and scope:** The comparison of what the legacy code actually does with what was intended, for the entire A02 scope of the Master System Investigation Index: the 58 A02 paths and every flow by which an A02 setting is read, validated, defaulted, transformed, overridden, ignored, hashed, snapshotted or consumed, at the fixed baseline. It audits the STUDY's 22 concerns and the A02 scope beyond them, records each result (MATCH, ISSUE or UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) with its intent source and code evidence, gives the A02 disposition of the A01 handoffs, and reviews the STUDY's ten questions for Rama; it decides nothing.
**Source:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (Source Inventory, SRC-10), read from the local read-only clone on 06-Nov-2026 and 08-Nov-2026; nothing in it was written or run.
**Related documents:** Documents 04, 06, 07, 08 and 10; the Master System Investigation Index; the Decision Register, D-12, D-17, D-21, D-23 to D-25, D-28 and D-30; ChatGPT's A02 investigation direction of 06-Nov-2026 (not kept as a file, D-17); the A02 STUDY (`A02_STUDY_2026-11-06.md`) and its evidence; `evidence/A02_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-06.md`; the A01 AUDIT, the A01 RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD (section 15) and the Audit Findings Register.

## 1. Purpose, scope, exclusions, baseline and method

### 1.1 Purpose

This is the AUDIT of topic A02 (Document 04, section 4, step 6; Document 07, section 5). For every A02 area and flow it compares the actual behaviour, established by the STUDY and re-checked here against the code, with the intended behaviour found in the admitted sources, and records matches, issues and UNKNOWN items (Document 04, section 5.4). It classifies; it does not decide. Every issue is raised with Rama after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5); no treatment and no V2 design is proposed here.

### 1.2 Scope and paths

The scope is the STUDY's (its sections 1.2 and 3): the 58 A02 paths at the baseline (the 50 YAML and CSV files under `config/` other than `config/cron_registry.yaml`, `core/config_loader.py`, `core/config_validator.py`, `core/config_auditor.py`, `core/config_snapshotter.py`, `strategies/schema.py`, `strategies/loader.py`, `.env.example` by key name only, and `scripts/revert_temp_config.py`), with every consumer's call site and the value it receives. Section 2 shows the coverage by path and by flow.

### 1.3 Exclusions

- Consumers' business logic belongs to the owner topics; this AUDIT records the call site, the value received and the owner, and hands the rest over (sections 5.5 to 9.5 and 10).
- Paper-only paths are marked and not audited (Document 08, section 2).
- A01's findings are not re-classified: where A02 has its own part (its file, key, schema or module), that part is recorded here and cross-referenced; where it has nothing to add, a pointer row is given (section 4).
- Credential values, the history of credential-shaped values and their exposure are outside A02 (Rama's instruction of 08-Nov-2026, relayed in ChatGPT's review of the same day): no credential-validity, history or exposure investigation is made, and no such point is put to Rama or handed to another topic. Credential variable names and their loading are audited where they are configuration (for example A01 C12).
- No repair of the legacy system, no treatment and no V2 design.

### 1.4 Baseline

Every line reference is to commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (D-21). Before and after the work, `git rev-parse HEAD` in the clone returned it and `git status --short` was empty, on 06-Nov-2026 and again on 08-Nov-2026.

### 1.5 Method

- Rama approved the method on 06-Nov-2026 ("Use helpers, as in A01"). The audit was done in six parts by AI helpers under the same read-only rules as the STUDY: the intent sources (the evidence file `evidence/A02_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-06.md`), and five audit parts, each with its own prefix: L (`core/config_loader.py`), V (the validator, the auditor, the W0 snapshot, the hashes and change detection), T (strategy configuration), F (the files as files, environment variables, credentials and sensitive locations, the revert script) and K (every configured key of `system_config.yaml`, `broker_limits.yaml`, `broker_costs.yaml` and `slippage_model.yaml` from file to consumer). Each part was given the STUDY concerns of its area (section 2.3) and the A01 handoffs of its area (section 4). The helpers' runs stopped on 06-Nov-2026 at a usage limit and resumed from where they stopped on 08-Nov-2026.
- Each part re-read every cited line at the baseline with `git show`, `git grep`, `git log` and `git blame`, and with its own scripts (Python's `ast` on the source text, `yaml.safe_load` and `yaml.compose` on the configuration text), kept outside the clone and run with `python3 -I`. Library behaviour (pydantic, PyYAML, python-dotenv, the standard library) was tested on synthetic inputs only and is INTERPRETATION. Tests were read as documents only. Nothing in the clone was written, run or imported.
- Intended behaviour was sought in every admitted source: the configuration files' own headers and comments, the schema's field comments and validator messages, module docstrings and comments, `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` (CL1 to CL6, S1 to S15 and the other configuration decisions), the configuration and strategy guides, the deployment guide, the TEMP tracker, the system map, the audit and decision documents, the module instructions, tests (as documents), commit messages and the existing technical records. Every such source is HISTORICAL. Where sources conflict, both are recorded and none is chosen; where no source states the intent, the record says "not established" and audits the code on its own (Document 04, section 5.3).
- Claude assembled the parts into this document. Two items recorded by both parts F and T were merged (section 1.8). One point that parts F and K each assigned to the other, so that neither recorded it, was checked by Claude against the baseline source and recorded at assembly (X-01, section 9.8). Claude re-read the code of the two KNOWN DEFECT records and of X-01, and checked the items that need Rama and the register entries against the records.
- After ChatGPT's review of 08-Nov-2026 the corrections of the revision history were made, and a separate helper that had written none of the parts checked every corrected record, every record whose result or count changed, every cross-topic handoff and the line references of the registers against the code and the index (section 14). It found eight points (V-08's basis not named; a count in section 6.6; the owners of three handoffs; the intended-behaviour cells of four register rows; the C10c wording of section 4; a leftover clause in section 6.7) and two borderline records (L-15, F-15); all are corrected, F-15 by reclassification as UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. The other records rest on their parts' own re-reading of the code and have not had a second-helper check.

### 1.6 Record fields, results, classifications and the KNOWN DEFECT rule

- Record fields (Document 04, section 5.4; Document 10, section 7): area and flow; issue category (Document 04, section 5.3); intended behaviour and its source (path and lines or key, with its date), or "not established"; the actual code path (path, function or class, lines); the actual behaviour and the effective value; the evidence; the result and its classification; the severity or impact where established; the owner topic; the cross-reference to the STUDY concern and to any A01 item; and whether Rama's intent or decision is needed. Issues and UNKNOWN items are blocks (sections 5.3 to 9.3, and 9.8); matches are table rows with the same information in short form (sections 5.4 to 9.4).
- Results, as in the A01 AUDIT (its section 1.6): MATCH (the code does what the sources intend); ISSUE (a difference from intended behaviour; a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way; or, where intent is not established, a defect or weakness that the code itself demonstrates, of a kind that Document 04, section 5.3 lists, the basis being named in the record); UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (the answer needs runtime evidence that does not survive, or intent is not established and the code demonstrates no such defect or weakness). A lack of intent evidence alone does not make an ISSUE. Two further kinds are not counted: INFORMATION (a configuration fact recorded for the description, with no A02 intent source, that is only a privacy or security concern: F-18) and WITHDRAWN (F-19, by Rama's instruction of 08-Nov-2026, relayed in ChatGPT's review of the same day). An item with a MATCH part and an ISSUE part is counted as ISSUE. An item is a MATCH with a recorded conflict only where a later-dated source, or a source that names the earlier one, replaces the conflicting source. A finding inside another topic's code is a handoff and is not counted as an A02 ISSUE; A02 records and counts its own part.
- Classifications (Document 04, section 6): FACT, INTERPRETATION, HISTORICAL, KNOWN DEFECT, OPEN QUESTION and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- The KNOWN DEFECT rule is the A01 AUDIT's, section 1.6, applied unchanged: (a) the code implements a mechanism for the case; (b) by the language semantics that mechanism fails for certain whenever the case occurs; (c) the case is one that the code or an intent source says it handles, and it can arise under the documented set-up (an IST-configured host running the documented options with the committed input, or a malformed input that a validation step exists to reject); (d) the outcome contradicts every intent source found. Each record that gives or considers KNOWN DEFECT states which conditions hold and which fails. Whether a KNOWN DEFECT occurred in production is always UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.

### 1.7 Identifiers and conventions

- L-nn, V-nn, T-nn, F-nn and K-nn are this AUDIT's items in parts L (section 5), V (section 6), T (section 7), F (section 8) and K (section 9); X-01 was added at assembly (section 9.8). Within each part the issue and UNKNOWN items are numbered first and the match rows after them. They are not the legacy labels (for example S1 to S15, CL1 to CL6, CV1 to CV3, P17, W0, NI-6) of the legacy documents.
- Inside sections 5 to 9, a reference to "section 1" to "section 7" without a document name means that part's own subsection (for example, inside section 7, "section 5" is section 7.5). Every other section reference names its document, or is to this AUDIT where the number has a dot and is outside 5 to 9 (for example section 10).
- IS-, IC-, CD- and RS-numbers are those of the evidence file `evidence/A02_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-06.md` (its sources, conflicts between sources, statements that only the code contradicts and statements attributed to Rama or the operator); the short names of legacy documents (for example LOCKED-YAML, CFG-GUIDE, STRAT-GUIDE, DEPLOY-GUIDE, TEMP-TRACKER, SMAP, M27, P14) are those of its section 1.3. "13.2 item n" is the STUDY's concern n, and "STUDY question n" its question n (section 15).
- Security: credential values, tokens, account identifiers, personal names, e-mail addresses, IP addresses, fingerprints and absolute home paths are never written; per-account variable names are written with `<ACCOUNT_ID>`, and passages that hold such material are cited by path and line only.
- Line references are written "L123" or "L123 to L130"; dates as 2026-11-08 or 08-Nov-2026.

Part K's nine coverage rows (eight sections or keys with nothing to match, one paper-only) are not counted. Pointer rows to A01 items (sections 6.4.2 and 8.4, and the S-39 row of section 7.2) are not counted.
- KNOWN DEFECT, provisional until the review and the issue discussion: T-01 (`validate_strategy` lets a `UnicodeDecodeError` from a non-UTF-8 file and a `TypeError` from a non-string top-level key escape instead of raising `ConfigSchemaError`, against S8; latent with the committed files) and F-20 (the revert script's "TEMP" test is a plain substring test: at the baseline it matches the word "ATTEMPT", so it reports six active entries in a file with no TEMP marker, and after an apply it matches its own "REVERTED from TEMP" comment, so the re-scan it asks for can never be clean). Each record states the four conditions; the other parts record why KNOWN DEFECT was considered and not given.
- By key (part K): of the 362 leaves of the four files audited key by key, 261 match, 96 are in ISSUE items and 5 are paper-only (part K, section 9.6).
- Locked decisions S1 to S15 (part T): MATCH for S1, S2, S3, S7, S12, S14 and S15; ISSUE for S4, S5, S6, S8, S9, S10, S11 and S13 (part T, section 7.6).
- Findings that need Rama's intent or decision to classify or to settle a conflict: 10 (F-22, T-08, T-09, T-10, T-11, V-08, V-14, K-03, K-07 for the SL-offset part, K-34), in 10 Open Issues Register entries, A02-01 to A02-05 and A02-08 to A02-12. A02-01 (F-22) has three subtopics: the daily loss limit (0.03) and `gap_fade_long.min_score` (0) are answered by evidence, and only the revert procedure is put to Rama; A02-08 (V-08) holds two questions (the delivery counterparts; the other coverage). Rama is asked 11 questions in all (section 12(b)). Every other ISSUE goes to Rama for treatment after ChatGPT's review.

## 5. Part L — The central loader and schema (`core/config_loader.py`)

Inside this part, a reference to "section 1" to "section 7" without a document name means this part's own section 5.1 to 5.7.

### 5.1 Scope and method of this part

- **Scope.** The whole of `core/config_loader.py` at trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (2,516 lines; last changed by `5f75c9b`, 2026-09-10): the module docstring and CL1 to CL6; the 65 models, their 458 fields (289 with a code default) and 97 validators, each validator message checked against its code; the `load_all` flow (registry, presence, read, hash, parse, normalise, validate, return); defaults and precedence; error behaviour and classes; caching and import-time state (L2449); the special-session schema (L1992); the MIS timing check (L109 to L137, literal poll interval at L131); `SmtpConfig.resolved_password`; commit `5f75c9b`; the call-site handling of the 26 `load_all` call sites in 21 files; the consumers that build models from code defaults. Out of scope here: the auditor's groups, the validator, the snapshotter, the strategy loader, the key-to-consumer map and the file values against locked decisions (other A02 parts); consumers' internals (their owners, section 5).
- **Baseline.** Before and after the work, `git rev-parse HEAD` returned the baseline commit and `git status --short` was empty. Nothing in the clone was written, imported or run.
- **Method.** Every line of the module read at the baseline. Intent sought in: the module and class docstrings, field comments and validator messages; the comments of the eight registry files; `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` (LOCKED-YAML; file 2026-07-22, CL1 to CL6 at L728 to L791, and every decision the schema's comments name: G4, G7, P1, P2, P11b, P12, P14, P15, P17, P9b, Q1, PR8, EOD12, TG12, AW7, AW11, RC17, CC9, `config_honor_principle`, `fail_fast_no_silent`); the module instructions in `docs/web_claude/01_module_instructions/` that define field labels (OM14, KS7, WR14, SP15, RE13; 2026-04-17); `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` (2026-07-28); `docs/audit/system_audit_14jun2026.md` (AUDIT-14JUN, F30); `docs/audit/clock_dependency_class_26jul2026.md` (2026-07-26); `docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` (FRULES 3.8); `core/exceptions.py`; `tests/unit/test_config_loader.py` (file 2026-08-27; read as a document only); commit messages (`c303e04`, `6f00b4d`, `5455ced`, `5f75c9b`) and `git blame` for line dates. A01's records C10a, C10b, C10c, C12, C35, M-19, M-20 and M-24 were read and are cross-referenced, not duplicated.
- **Own scripts** (in the A02 work folder, run with `python3 -I`, legacy text read with `git show`, never imported): `L_defaults.py` (AST of the module against `yaml.safe_load` of the eight baseline files: every code default compared with the file value); `L_dupkeys.py` (`yaml.compose` of the eight files: duplicate mapping keys); `L_probe.py` (pydantic 2.13.5 and PyYAML 6.0.3 on synthetic models and text only; pinned versions are pydantic 2.13.0 and PyYAML 6.0.3, `requirements.txt` L6 to L7). Library behaviour is INTERPRETATION.
- **Correction applied (from the independent check of the STUDY, A01 C10c).** In the `main.py` boot the holiday guard reads the holiday file before `load_all` (`main.py` L2100 to L2152; `utils/holiday_guard.py` L44 to L62) and tolerates only a missing file (L2151 to L2152); an unreadable or malformed holiday file ends the start there with exit 2, before `load_all`. Verified at the baseline; L-01 and the error rows below are written accordingly.
- **Labels and results** follow RULES.md item 5 and AUDIT_RULES.md items 4 and 5; the KNOWN DEFECT rule is that of the A01 AUDIT, section 1.6. No sensitive value is reproduced; environment-variable names held in configuration are given as key names only.

The coordinator's C10c correction narrows the `main.py` effect to the missing-file case. |

The message naming `mis_squareoff.poll_interval_sec` is in `core/mis_squareoff_timing.py` L127 (A12), handed off (section 5). |

### 5.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### L-01 — The holiday file: one year, fixed at import from the host-local date, and required by every load

- **Area and flow:** the registry and import-time state; the holiday file in `load_all` (SOURCE → INPUT → VALIDATION).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Flow and state; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (1) the file name must not break at the year change: AUDIT-14JUN F30 (`docs/audit/system_audit_14jun2026.md` L152 to L154, 2026-06-13: "`nse_holidays_2026.yaml` hardcoded ... Will break on Jan 1 2027"), fixed by `c303e04` (2026-06-13, "F30: nse_holidays filename uses dynamic year (_date.today().year)"). (2) One time source, in IST: `core/time_authority.py` L5 to L7 and L96 to L97 ("No datetime.now() elsewhere"); FRULES 3.8 (`docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` L221 to L225, 2026-08-06: "IST throughout the project"). (3) All eight files are required and a failure stops the load: CL2 (LOCKED-YAML L741 to L749: "A startup failure is always better than runtime errors"); `load_all` docstring L2459 to L2463 (2026-04-17). (4) The operator adds the next year's file before 1 January: `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` L502 to L504 (2026-07-28: "Update it at year-end"); tests, read as documents (`tests/unit/test_config_loader.py` L1095 to L1210, file 2026-08-27): dated tripwires, "WHEN THIS GOES RED, THE FIX IS TO ADD THE FILE", and the remark that the file "is a HARD boot requirement serving a SOFT purpose"; `docs/audit/clock_dependency_class_26jul2026.md` L100 to L135 (2026-07-26: "LATENT, with a date-certain trigger ... Pinned, not fixed"). (5) Stale: the module docstring L33 and the section comment L2392 (2026-04-17) and CONFIG_GUIDE L503 name `nse_holidays_2026.yaml`. Against (3): the holiday guard's "missing holiday file: proceed with startup" (`main.py` L2152; A01). HISTORICAL throughout.
- **Actual code path:** `_CONFIG_FILES` L2441 to L2450, holiday entry L2449 (`f"nse_holidays_{_date.today().year}.yaml"`); `load_all` L2480 to L2488 (presence), L2490 to L2514; `HolidayEntry` and `NseHolidaysConfig` L2395 to L2413.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the name is built once, in a module-level tuple, when the module is first imported, from `date.today()` (host-local); the module uses no time authority; only that year's file is read; it is required like the other seven, with no fallback; nothing checks that the entries belong to that year, are unique or are weekdays; `holidays: []` passes. FACT (`git ls-tree config/`): only `config/nse_holidays_2026.yaml` exists. INTERPRETATION (Python import semantics): a process that imports the module with a host-local date in 2027 gets `ConfigMissingError` from every `load_all` call, so every one of the 26 call sites fails (`main.py` exit 5, L2197 to L2201; the alert watcher, the reconcile scripts, the pre-flight and the control tower, section 5); a process that imports before a year change and loads after it still asks for the earlier year's file. With the C10c correction: in the `main.py` boot the guard reads the same file first, so a missing file passes the guard and then fails here with exit 5, while an unreadable or malformed file ends the start at the guard with exit 2 and never reaches the loader (INTERPRETATION: the loader's `ConfigSchemaError` for the holiday file arises in `main.py` only if the file changes between L2100 and L2198; for scripts the loader is the first reader). Effective at the baseline file set: the 2026 file loads; from the first import on 1-Jan-2027 (host-local) no load succeeds until the 2027 file is added.
- **Evidence:** code read; `git blame` L2449 (`c303e04`); `git show -s c303e04`; F30 record; the clock-dependency record; tests read as documents; `git ls-tree`; `utils/holiday_guard.py` L44 to L62 and `main.py` L2098 to L2152 read for the correction.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (code, file set); INTERPRETATION (import semantics and consequences); sources conflict. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the F30 dynamic-year mechanism); (b) holds (with the baseline file set a 2027 import cannot find its file); (c) holds for the year change on an IST host with the committed files, while the host-local-against-IST part needs a non-IST host and is excluded; (d) fails: CL2 and the tests intend a missing required file to stop the load, and the documented procedure is to add the file before the year ends, so the outcome does not contradict every source.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none in 2026 at the baseline files; from the first 2027 import, every `load_all` caller fails until `nse_holidays_2027.yaml` is committed (`main.py` exit 5). The VM's time zone is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (A01 B-41).
- **Owner:** A02 (L2449, the registry, the schema); A01 (the guard and the boot).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 1; A01 C10a (A02's line L2449), C10b (the file requirement), C10c (the malformed file at the guard); STUDY section 15 question 8.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; whether a missing next-year file was meant to stop every process, and whether the year was meant to follow IST (STUDY question 8), bears on treatment.

##### L-02 — The MIS timing check at load uses the literal poll interval 5

- **Area and flow:** `trading_hours` validation, delegated to the MIS timing builder (VALIDATION / GUARD).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components; Failure handling.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the validator's docstring L111 to L116 (2026-08-28, `6f00b4d`): "FAIL CLOSED on any invalid MIS square-off timing ... so the schema and the runtime cannot disagree: there is ONE definition of the ordering invariant ... and of the margin floor"; `config/system_config.yaml` L96 to L102 (2026-08-28): the margin is "DERIVED" from "poll_interval_sec 5" and "A margin below poll_interval_sec is incoherent and FAILS CLOSED"; commit `6f00b4d` message ("a margin below the poll interval fails closed"); `core/mis_squareoff_timing.py` L37 ("The boot must FAIL CLOSED on this"; A12).
- **Actual code path:** `TradingHoursConfig._validate_mis_squareoff_timing` L109 to L137 (literal `poll_interval_sec=5` at L131; conversion to `ValueError` L135 to L136); `EodSquareoffConfig._validate_poll` L676 to L681 (only `>= 1`); the delegated rule `core/mis_squareoff_timing.py` L125 to L138; the runtime build `main.py` L3329 to L3337 (configured value at L3334) and the notifier L3343 to L3345.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: at load the margin floor compares `mis_squareoff_margin_sec` with 5, not with `eod_squareoff.poll_interval_sec`; no rule in this module or the auditor ties the two keys (grep). INTERPRETATION: the two checks agree only when the configured poll interval is 5. (i) A configured poll interval above the margin (for example 21 with margin 20) passes the load and fails at `main.py` L3329 with `MisSquareoffConfigError` (exit 2; A01 M-24). (ii) A configured poll interval below 5 with a margin between it and 5 (for example poll 2, margin 4) is refused at load (`ConfigSchemaError`, exit 5) although the stated rule `margin >= poll` holds. Effective at the baseline: poll 5 (YAML L361), margin 20 (L96): both checks pass; latent.
- **Evidence:** code read; `git blame` L131 (`6f00b4d`, 2026-08-28); `git show -s 6f00b4d`; grep for any rule relating the two keys (none).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (a load-time validator for the case); (b) holds (with a literal 5 the floor cannot see the configured interval); (c) holds (an incoherent margin is a malformed input the floor exists to reject); (d) fails: in case (i) the boot still fails closed before trading, as `core/mis_squareoff_timing.py` L37 requires (A01 M-24 reached the same reading); only the docstring's "cannot disagree" and the load-time locus are contradicted.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent at the file values; case (i) gives exit 2 at L3329 instead of exit 5 at load; case (ii) refuses a configuration the runtime rule accepts.
- **Owner:** A02 (L131); A12 (`core/mis_squareoff_timing.py`); A01 (the exit code).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 2; A01 M-24.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### L-03 — `special_sessions`: type only, and the documented example fails the load

- **Area and flow:** `SystemConfig.special_sessions` (L1992), schema (VALIDATION).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** field comment L1992 (2026-05-18, `4c99692`): "FIX-094: date -> {market_open, market_close, eod_squareoff_time}"; `config/system_config.yaml` L114 to L116 (2026-05-17, `ee91659`): "Format: date (YYYY-MM-DD) -> {...}", "Uncomment and configure when special session is scheduled", with the commented example L118 to L122 (unquoted date key, quoted times); YAML L5 (2026-04-17): "All time values are "HH:MM" strings interpreted as IST (P1)"; `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` L206 to L211 (2026-07-28): "Uncomment and fill the date block when one is scheduled"; YAML L86 to L89 (2026-08-28, a stated known limitation: the MIS cutoff is not moved, "the ordering invariant will not detect the divergence"); the CFG-1 principle of the window-ordering validator (L141 to L154: catch operator typos; "a loud, early boot failure").
- **Actual code path:** L1992 (a mapping of strings to mappings of strings, or `None`, default `None`); no validator in this module or in `core/config_auditor.py` (grep).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: any string keys; an inner mapping of any string keys to string values; no date format, key set, HH:MM, ordering, or relation to `service_window_end`, the MIS cutoff or the holiday list. Baseline value: `null`. INTERPRETATION (verified on synthetic text, PyYAML 6.0.3 and pydantic 2.13.5): the example uncommented as written gives a date-typed key, which the string type refuses (`string_type` at the key), so the load fails with `ConfigSchemaError` (exit 5 in `main.py`); an unquoted time such as 18:15 becomes the integer 1095 and is refused; quoted but malformed entries pass and fail later in `main.py` `_load_special_sessions` (A01 C35, exit 2).
- **Evidence:** code read; grep; `L_probe.py`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION. KNOWN DEFECT not given: (a) fails (no validation mechanism for the entries; the type check works as declared).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline (`null`); following the documented "uncomment" step with the example as written stops every load.
- **Owner:** A02 (schema; YAML comment); A01 (runtime parse).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 3; A01 C35 (A02 answers C35's open point: the commented key as written is refused by the schema at load).
- **Rama:** not needed; A01's R11 covers the intent for special sessions.

##### L-04 — "No defaults, all required" against 289 code defaults

- **Area and flow:** schema-wide defaults (DEFAULT → EFFECTIVE VALUE).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** module docstring L39 (2026-04-17): "Does not fill in defaults for missing keys (CL3: extra="forbid", all required)"; comment L2277 (2026-04-17): "Locked: P9b (all weights and thresholds config-driven; nothing hardcoded)" and `config/scoring_weights.yaml` L4. Against these: CL3's own decision text (LOCKED-YAML L751 to L760) speaks of typos that "would otherwise silently use a schema default", which presumes defaults; later field comments choose defaults on purpose: L94 to L98 (2026-07-25) and L99 to L103 (2026-08-28) "DEFAULTED so that under extra="forbid" each half of a deploy is independently safe"; L576 to L578 (entry throttle "All default OFF"); L2253 ("default applied if yaml omits the block"); the shadow modules' "ONE default-off flag" docstrings.
- **Actual code path:** all 65 models (AST).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: 289 of 458 fields have a code default. FACT (own comparison `L_defaults.py`, agreeing with STUDY 4.4): 38 key occurrences (24 distinct keys) are absent from the baseline files and take the default; 31 defaults differ from the file value: 13 in `system_config.yaml` L21 to L357 (as STUDY 4.4 lists) and 18 more in L359 onward, `scoring_weights.yaml` and `scan_webhook_map.yaml` (3 of them paper-only). INTERPRETATION: deleting any of those 31 keys changes behaviour without an error; for example, deleting `excluded_symbols` empties the five-symbol exclusion list (default `[]`, L1993; no source states the intent of that default), while deleting `alerts.smtp.password_env` makes the load fail (both password sources empty, L923 to L933). The v3 scoring thresholds (L2315 to L2317) are code defaults despite "nothing hardcoded" (present in the file). `extra="forbid"` catches a misspelt key, not a deleted one.
- **Evidence:** AST; `L_defaults.py`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (counts); INTERPRETATION (effect of a deletion). The code follows the later field-level choices; the docstring L39 and the P9b comment were not updated (a comment mismatch, A01 AUDIT section 1.6).
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline files.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 4; STUDY 4.4 (extended: 31 differing defaults across the eight files, not 13).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### L-05 — CL6's split rule is not applied

- **Area and flow:** module structure.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** CL6 (LOCKED-YAML L782 to L791): "If the file exceeds 600 lines, split into core/config_schemas/ package ... The 600-line trigger gives a concrete split point that will never be hit by 8 small schemas"; docstring L22 to L23 (2026-04-17).
- **Actual code path:** the whole module.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: 2,516 lines and 65 model classes; no `core/config_schemas/` exists (`git ls-tree core/`).

##### L-10 — Lax type coercion against "NO coercion" and two type guards

- **Area and flow:** type validation of every field (VALIDATION).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Logic and calculation.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `LeverageMapConfig` docstring L295 to L298 (2026-08-27, `52ccb4f`): "A missing, null, zero, negative, non-finite or out-of-range value is REJECTED ... There is NO silent default and NO coercion"; module docstring L6 (2026-04-17): "strict Pydantic v2 schema"; CL3's title "Strict schemas", whose decision text defines it as `extra='forbid'` (LOCKED-YAML L751 to L760), as does `requirements.txt` L7 (2026-04-17: "CL3: strict schema validation (extra="forbid")"); the explicit bool refusal in `SlippageOverridesConfig._fractions_in_range` L1740 (2026-06-20); the bool guard on the margin in `core/mis_squareoff_timing.py` L121 ("expected an integer").
- **Actual code path:** no `ConfigDict` sets `strict` (all 65 models); L1736 to L1746; `mis_squareoff_margin_sec: int` L107.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: pydantic's default lax mode. INTERPRETATION (pydantic 2.13.5 on synthetic models; the pinned 2.13.0 was not tested): a float field accepts a YAML bool (`true` becomes 1.0) and a numeric string (`"5"` becomes 5.0); an int field accepts `true` (1) and 5.0 (5); a bool field accepts `"yes"`, `"off"` and 1. So `leverage_map.DELIVERY: true` loads as 1.0 and passes the pin; `risk.max_open_positions: true` loads as 1 and passes `>= 1`; a `true` in a slippage-override map is coerced to 1.0 before L1740 runs, so its bool test can never fire and the override takes the widest allowed fraction; the margin's bool guard is likewise never reached from YAML (the coerced 1 is refused by the floor anyway). Numbers, dates and bools are not coerced to `str`, which is why unquoted times are refused.
- **Evidence:** code read; `L_probe.py`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (no strict mode); INTERPRETATION (library semantics). CL3's "strict" means `extra="forbid"`, which the code meets (match L-21); the "NO coercion" statement and the L1740 guard are contradicted. KNOWN DEFECT considered for the L1740 guard and not given: (a), (c) and (d) hold; (b) rests on pydantic's lax-mode semantics, tested at 2.13.5 only, not on language semantics, so it is not established.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline files (all values well typed, STUDY 4.4 static check).
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 6.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### L-11 — NaN and infinity pass validators whose messages state a range

- **Area and flow:** range validators of float fields (VALIDATION / GUARD).
- **Issue category:** Logic and calculation; Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** each validator's own message, which states its rule; `_validate_finite_leverage` docstring L236 to L240 (2026-08-27): "a range check alone lets NaN through ... Both must be named and refused" (applied to leverage only).
- **Actual code path:** rules written as "raise if v < x" or "raise if v <= x": L357 to L363 (`sl_limit_offset_pct`, message ">= 0 and < 0.10"); L374 to L380 (`emergency_exit_buffer_pct`); L595 to L600 (`drain_poll_sec`, "> 0"); L637 to L642 (`per_ip_refill_per_sec`, ">= 0"); L1068 to L1097 (four `order_reconciler` floats); L1131 to L1136 (`min_free_disk_gb`, ">= 0.1"); L1504 to L1507 (`rr_floor`, `exec_budget`, "> 0"); L1572 to L1573 (`watchlist.poll_interval_sec`); L2155 to L2159 (nine broker cost rates, ">= 0"; the `== 0` tests at L2161 and L2166 also pass NaN); L2209 to L2242 (`rate_limit_backoff`: delays, multiplier, `max >= initial`); L551 to L561 (`flat_value_rs > 0` when sizing is off). Paper-only L1637 to L1668: not traced. Refused: leverage (L235 to L247); rules written as "require in range" (for example L370, L385, L511, L612, L694, L703, L725); the drift thresholds through the ordering rule (L1906 to L1918); the allocator through L1377.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (Python float semantics: every comparison with NaN is false; PyYAML reads `.nan` and `.inf` as floats and pydantic float fields accept them, probe): NaN passes every listed rule while violating its stated range; +infinity passes the ">= x" and "> 0" rules (for example `min_free_disk_gb: .inf`). Float fields without a validator accept both. FACT (grep): no `.nan` or `.inf` in the eight baseline files.
- **Evidence:** code read; `L_probe.py`; grep of the files.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. INTERPRETATION. KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a), (c) and (d) hold; (b): the comparisons fail by Python semantics, but whether a NaN reaches them depends on pydantic's float handling at the pinned version (tested at 2.13.5 only), so it is not established for the whole chain.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline files.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 6.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### L-16 — The docstring calls the module the loader for all non-strategy YAML files

- **Area and flow:** module purpose (SOURCE).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual.

- **Actual code path:** `_CONFIG_FILES` L2441 to L2450.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (`git ls-tree config/`): 15 top-level YAML files; `load_all` reads 8; not read: `cron_registry.yaml` (A16), `expected_managers.yaml`, `index_universe.yaml`, `preflight.yaml`, `security.yaml`, `strategy_direction_registry.yaml`, `symbol_aliases.yaml`, each read by its own consumer with its own parsing and defaults (STUDY 4.1, 10.3).
- **Evidence:** `git ls-tree`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (a comment mismatch). FACT.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none on behaviour.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 items 13 and 20 (other parts).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### L-17 — Validator messages that describe a stricter format than the code checks

- **Area and flow:** HH:MM and date parsing (VALIDATION).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** message L1558 "watchlist entry time must be HH:MM"; message L2408 "Expected YYYY-MM-DD string for date"; YAML L5 ("HH:MM"); FRULES 3.8 ("ISO format: YYYY-MM-DD everywhere").
- **Actual code path:** `WatchlistConfig._validate_hhmm` L1551 to L1559; `TradingHoursConfig._hhmm` L158 to L160 (no message of its own); `HolidayEntry._parse_iso_date` L2401 to L2408.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (Python): `int()` accepts "5", " 09" and "+9", so "9:5", " 09:20" and "+9:30" pass the watchlist check and `_hhmm` (for `market_open`, `market_close`, `entry_start`, `eod_entry_cutoff`, `service_window_end`); a malformed trading-hours time is refused with Python's own text (for example from tuple unpacking), not a message naming the rule; from Python 3.11 `date.fromisoformat` also accepts other ISO forms such as "20260126" (verified on 3.13; the deployment guide names 3.11, `docs/06_deployment_guide.md` L26, HISTORICAL), and for a malformed string its error text, not L2408's, is shown. At the baseline every value is a two-digit HH:MM or an ISO date.
- **Evidence:** code read; `L_probe.py` (date parsing).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor). INTERPRETATION.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline files.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 7.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### L-18 — UNKNOWN: whether `5f75c9b` and the 03-Sep MIS values were ever in force

- **Area and flow:** the post-deployment change (commit `5f75c9b`) and its configured values.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** commit `5f75c9b` (2026-09-10) and comment L654 to L665; YAML L366 to L377.
- **Actual code path:** L666 to L667; `_validate_market_protection_percent` L709 to L731.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the code matches its stated unit and bounds (match L-31). HISTORICAL (Decision Register D-21, as cited by STUDY 1.8): the last recorded deployment is `7d4970a` (02-Sep-2026); `5455ced` (03-Sep) and `5f75c9b` (10-Sep) are later.
- **Evidence:** `git log`; STUDY 1.8.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: no runtime evidence survives (Document 06, section 4.2).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established.
- **Owner:** A02; A12 and A04 for the consumers.
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 1.8; 13.3 item 1.
- **Rama:** not needed.

## 6. Part V — The validator, the auditor, the W0 snapshot and change detection

Inside this part, a reference to "section 1" to "section 7" without a document name means this part's own section 6.1 to 6.7.

### 6.1 Scope and method of this part

- **Scope (part V).** `core/config_validator.py` (registration, `get`, `validate_all`, `strict`, drift, docstring CV1 to CV4, the call sites `main.py` L2205 and L4164); `core/config_auditor.py` (every group A to G and every code, severities, `audit()` and `groups`, `STARTUP_GROUPS` against its docstring, the `capital` parameter, its callers: `SystemConfig._cross_field_sanity_checks` in `core/config_loader.py` L2056 to L2086, `main.py`'s strategy-aware re-runs L3436 to L3465, pre-flight `config_sanity` and the control tower; exception handling; what it does not inspect; group B against the schema); `core/config_snapshotter.py` (the W0 snapshot: content, exclusions, redaction, canonical JSON, hash, duplicate check, storage in `core/schema.sql` `config_snapshots`, logging, docstring claims, the call site `main.py` L2593 to L2614); the CL4 boot hash (`file_hashes`, raw bytes, the eight-file set) and the configuration side of its comparison by `utils/startup_checks.py::check_config_hash` (A01's code); the outcomes for added, changed, removed, missing, unreadable and invalid files; the exposure paths of configuration content (paths only).

### 6.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

#### 6.3.1 The key-usage validator

##### V-01 — The key-usage validator is never fed: every key is reported unaccessed and drift is never detected

- **Area and flow:** configuration validation at boot; `main.py` L2205 registers every leaf after `load_all`, subsystems are built, `main.py` L4164 calls `validate_all(strict=False)`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `core/config_validator.py` module docstring (`419c430`, 2026-05-02): "Enforces that ALL config values from YAML are actually used by modules … Fails startup if any drift detected between loaded config and usage" (L5 to L7); CV1 "Runtime tracking. ConfigValidator.track(key, value) called whenever a config value is read. At startup end, validate_all() asserts all loaded keys were tracked at least once" (L17 to L19); CV2 mandatory injection, no defaults in module constructors (L20 to L21); CV3 "Any untracked config key → ConfigValidationError → exit(1)" (L22 to L23); usage: each module `__init__` reads through `config_validator.get(...)` (L30 to L34); `core/exceptions.py` L74 to L85 (`ConfigValidationError`, "Raised by: core/config_validator.py at startup validation gate"); `main.py` L2204 "CV1: Register all config values for usage tracking (post-paper audit)" and L4163 "CV3 … (non-strict for gradual rollout)" (both `419c430`); `validate_all` docstring L226 to L227 ("If False, only warn (for gradual rollout)"); test `tests/core/test_config_validator.py` L148 to L155 ("raises on drift regardless of strict flag"). HISTORICAL: `docs/pending_skipped_items.txt` L4 to L9 (2026-04-28) lists "ConfigValidator implementation - Pydantic schema + mandatory injection" and "Startup assertion: loaded config == actually-used config" as pending; `docs/audit/bk8_dynamic_sites_classified_04aug2026.md` L54 to L80 (2026-08-04) calls the walker "presumably load-bearing"; `docs/audit/BOOT_PROOF_75e637c_26-Aug-2026.md` L159 (2026-08-26) records a boot-window WARNING "CONFIG_UNACCESSED: 388 config keys never read" at commit `75e637c` (a legacy record of a log line, not runtime evidence available to this project).
- **Actual code path:** `ConfigValidator.get` L104 to L142 (the only code that sets `accessed`, L121 and L136); `register_all_from_app_config` L144 to L183; `get_usage_report` L185 to L219; `validate_all` L221 to L271; singleton L274; call sites `main.py` L60, L2205, L4164.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (`git grep`, non-test code): nothing calls `get()` on the singleton or on any instance, and no module imports `ConfigValidator`; tests call `get()` on their own instances only. HISTORICAL (`git log -S 'config_validator.get('`): the only commit containing the text is `419c430`, the docstring itself; no consumer has ever called it. FACT: CV2 is not enforced by any code and CV4 is "Not implemented in v1" (L24 to L25). INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): every registered key stays `accessed=False`, so the unaccessed list is the whole registry and the drift list is always empty (drift needs `accessed`, L204 to L207); each boot that reaches L4164 logs one WARNING `CONFIG_UNACCESSED: N config keys never read: [first 10]` (L242 to L255) and one INFO "0/N keys accessed, 0 drift" (L268 to L271) and never raises. The report therefore cannot tell a used key from an unused one, and a value that a module overrides with its own default is never detected. The `ConfigValidationError` raises at L250 and L263 exist but are unreachable at the baseline: the unaccessed raise because `main.py` passes `strict=False`, the drift raise because nothing is accessed. N is UNKNOWN (V-15).
- **Evidence:** the module in full; `git grep` for `config_validator`, `ConfigValidator`, `.get(`, `track(`; `git log -S`; the tests as documents (L22 to L225); the three HISTORICAL documents.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT (no caller) and INTERPRETATION (consequence). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the unaccessed and drift checks of `validate_all`); (b) fails: the mechanism works when fed (the tests drive it on their own instances); what is missing is the CV1 tracking calls, a requirement the code does not implement, which section 1.6 of the A01 AUDIT classes as an ISSUE; (c) and (d) are not reached.
- **Severity or impact where established:** the validator's stated purpose (find unused configuration, fail startup on drift) is not achieved at the baseline; one WARNING listing ten key names reaches the boot log on every boot that reaches L4164; A01 M-24's `ConfigValidationError` exit-1 path cannot occur at the baseline (INTERPRETATION).
- **Owner:** A02 (the validator); A01 (the two call sites).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 9; A01 W-17 (MATCH for the call site, not re-decided), M-20, M-24; STUDY section 7.4 and question 10.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; whether the non-strict warning was meant to be the end state (STUDY question 10) bears on treatment.

##### V-02 — The validator's walk registers less than "ALL config values"

- **Area and flow:** `register_all_from_app_config`, called once at `main.py` L2205.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** module docstring L5 ("ALL config values from YAML"); method docstring L146 to L149 ("register every config value with its dotted path key") (`419c430`, 2026-05-02).
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_validator.py` L154 to L183.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: only `system`, `broker_costs`, `broker_limits`, `slippage` and `scoring` are walked (L179 to L183); the `scan_webhook_map`, `chartink_scanners` and `nse_holidays` sub-configs (`core/config_loader.py` L2431 to L2433) are not, nor `file_hashes`. Lists are walked only for pydantic items (L172 to L175), so every scalar list (for example `excluded_symbols`, `slippage_bands`, `alerts.smtp.to_addresses`, `scoring.v3_gate_steps`) is not registered at all. A dict field registers each entry, but a nested plain dict (for example each broker of `product_map`) is one leaf, and an empty dict registers nothing (L165 to L171). `register_expected` overwrites any earlier entry for a key (L102).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; the AppConfig fields (`core/config_loader.py` L2426 to L2434).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the baseline, because nothing is accessed (V-01) (INTERPRETATION); if the validator were fed, keys of three registry files and every scalar list could never be reported unaccessed or drifted.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 9; STUDY section 7.4.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### V-03 — The validator's docstrings describe a method, a gate and a usage that the code does not have

- **Area and flow:** the validator's documentation against its code and its call at `main.py` L4164.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** CV1 L17 to L19 names `ConfigValidator.track(key, value)`; CV3 L22 to L23 "Any untracked config key → ConfigValidationError → exit(1)"; usage L37 `config_validator.validate_all(app_config)`; class docstring L72 "Thread-safe for read tracking (via get())"; `validate_all` docstring L229 to L230 ("Raises: ConfigValidationError: In strict mode when keys are unaccessed"). Conflicting sources of the same commit (`419c430`, 2026-05-02): `validate_all` L226 to L227 ("If False, only warn (for gradual rollout)") and `main.py` L4163 ("non-strict for gradual rollout").
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_validator.py` L68 to L271; `main.py` L4164.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the class has no `track` method; the default is `strict=False` (L221) and `main.py` passes `strict=False`, so an untracked key gives a WARNING, not CV3's exit(1); the module imports no `threading` and holds no lock. INTERPRETATION (Python call semantics): the documented usage `validate_all(app_config)` would bind the configuration object to `strict`, and a truthy object selects the raising branch (L248 to L253). INTERPRETATION: `access_count += 1` and the list appends in `get()` are not synchronised; this has no effect while `get()` has no caller. FACT: the drift branch raises whatever `strict` is (L257 to L266), as L7 and the test L148 to L155 intend, but the "Raises" section names only the strict case.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git grep` for `track`; the test file as a document.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (stale and conflicting documentation); FACT for the texts, INTERPRETATION for the binding and the synchronisation. CV3 conflicts with the two "gradual rollout" statements of the same date; the code follows the latter, and no later source replaces CV3.
- **Severity or impact where established:** documentation only at the baseline; a caller who followed L37 would get strict mode.
- **Owner:** A02 (the docstrings); A01 (the call site, W-17).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 9; A01 W-17 (A01's MATCH for `main.py` L4163 to L4164 is not re-decided).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; which statement states the intended end state (CV3 or "gradual rollout") is STUDY question 10, for treatment.

#### 6.3.2 The configuration auditor

##### V-04 — The auditor's descriptions of its callers, startup groups, logging and context differ from the code

- **Area and flow:** `core/config_auditor.py` documentation against its own code and its callers.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** (a) `audit()` docstring L785 to L786: "the startup path runs ACEG" (`eb373c4`, 2026-06-25); against it, in the same commit, the `STARTUP_GROUPS` comment L806 to L810 (E "belongs in the 09:20 pre-flight email, not every process boot"), the loader docstring `core/config_loader.py` L2063 to L2069 ("A contradictions, C capital-relative, G cross-field") and the test `tests/unit/test_config_auditor.py` L558 to L561 ("must not emit B/D/E/F findings"). (b) Module docstring L8: "ONE rule engine, two callers". (c) L11 to L12 and `audit_system_config` L816 to L817: the startup caller "logs WARN/INFO findings"; against it the loader docstring L2065 ("logs the WARN findings"). (d) L14 to L15: pre-flight passes "the FULL context (capital + strategies + symbols + raw YAML)". (e) Group B docstring L331 to L332: "so it runs in the pre-flight context only". (f) B5's comment L368 labels it "A9 (V3 03.04)" (`536172c`, 2026-07-12). (g) `C_ok` message L528: "conc … < pos-cap …".
- **Actual code path:** `audit` L772 to L803; `STARTUP_GROUPS` L811; `audit_system_config` L814 to L818; `audit_app_config` L821 to L847; loader L2071 to L2086; callers `main.py` L3437 to L3438 and L3459 to L3460, `scripts/preflight/checks/config_sanity.py` L43 to L44, `ops/control_tower/aggregator.py` L161.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: (a) the startup subset is "ACG"; (b) there are four call sites (the loader's model validator, the two `main.py` re-runs, pre-flight and the control tower); (c) the loader logs WARN findings only (L2083 to L2084); INTERPRETATION: groups A, C and G produce only PASS, WARN and BLOCK, so at startup there is no INFO finding and the difference has no effect; (d) pre-flight passes `config_dir` and `strategies` only: no `known_symbols` (effect in V-07) and no `capital`, and `capital` is "reserved" (L780) and unused in the body (L788 to L803); (e) the control tower also runs group B (it passes `config_dir`); (f) "A9" is the V3 plan's label, also used by the loader at L2328, not a group-A code; (g) since NI-2 (`c146eb7`, 2026-08-22) C2 compares against concentration × the effective multiplier (L453 to L466), so the `C_ok` text states the earlier rule; INTERPRETATION: if every tier weight and `max_multiplier` were below 1, C2 could pass with the position cap below the concentration cap while `C_ok` says the opposite.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git blame` dates; the test as a document.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor; stale and conflicting documentation); FACT, with INTERPRETATION for (c) and (g). The startup constant and the sources that agree with it are of the same date as L786; no later source names and replaces L786.
- **Severity or impact where established:** documentation only, except (d), whose effect is V-07.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 8; STUDY section 7.3.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### V-05 — B5 is a BLOCK in a group documented as WARN-only, and it can fire only on raw values that the schema has already accepted

- **Area and flow:** group B in pre-flight (A20) and the control tower (A15); `ScoringConfig` at `load_all`.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components; Logic and calculation.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** module docstring L27 ("B. Single-source integrity . regression guards: a deleted key reappearing (WARN)") and `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` L562 to L563 ("Only group A can BLOCK") and L570 (B's worst verdict WARN), both 2026-06-25; `scripts/preflight/checks/config_sanity.py` L86 ("the only group that can BLOCK", 2026-06-25); B5's own comment L368 to L370 (2026-07-12): "config_loader also fail-fasts on this; the auditor surfaces it on the raw YAML in pre-flight".
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_auditor.py` L371 to L382; `core/config_loader.py` `ScoringConfig` L2298 to L2317 (`int` thresholds) and `_validate_threshold_ordering` L2326 to L2339; callers `config_sanity.py` L33 and `aggregator.py` L145 (both call `load_all` first).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: B5 compares the raw YAML values `hi > mid > lo` for the live and the `v3_` sets and gives BLOCK. FACT: the schema enforces the same ordering on the validated values at `load_all`, which both group-B callers run before the auditor; a file with a real ordering violation fails `load_all`, so pre-flight's seven rows show "auditor unavailable" and the control tower a critical "config failed to load", and B5 never runs. INTERPRETATION (pydantic lax mode, synthetic test; Python comparison): B5 can fire only where the raw and validated values differ. Quoted thresholds are coerced to `int` and pass the schema, but B5 compares the strings, and string order can give a BLOCK on a valid configuration (values with different digit counts); a mix of quoted and unquoted values raises `TypeError` at L377, which nothing in the auditor catches: pre-flight then reports all seven rows "auditor unavailable" (`config_sanity.py` L46 to L47, L63 to L64), and the control tower's call (L161) has no handler. A file changed between the two reads would also differ. FACT: a B5 BLOCK makes pre-flight's row B `_failed` with criticality WARN (L76 to L77, L92) and makes the control tower's status "critical" (L166). File values at the baseline: all six thresholds are unquoted integers in order (live 60, 65, 80; v3 50, 56, 75): no B5 finding.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; own file-value script; synthetic pydantic and comparison tests.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT for the conflict with L27, CONFIG_GUIDE L563 and `config_sanity.py` L86; INTERPRETATION for the reachability. B5 is later than the three sources and does not name them.
- **Severity or impact where established:** at the file values none; a quoted threshold can produce a false BLOCK, or blank the whole pre-flight Config Sanity section, while the trading boot loads normally.
- **Owner:** A02 (the rule); A20 and A15 (the rendering and handling).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 8; STUDY section 7.3 ("Group B and the schema").
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### V-06 — The auditor swallows errors in its probes, so the A5 boot gate can pass silently, and a skipped group B is shown as PASS

- **Area and flow:** exception handling inside `audit()` for every caller; the A5 gate at `main.py` L3458 to L3465.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling; Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** LOCKED-YAML `principles.fail_fast_no_silent` L434 to L437 (2026-04-17): "NO bare except, NO try/except/log/continue patterns that swallow errors"; `main.py` L3444 to L3450 (2026-07-27): A5 is re-run "and fail-fast before any capital is reserved"; the A5 comment L250 to L258 (why BLOCK); group F's NI-5 docstring L599 to L608 (2026-08-23): "A ROW THIS GUARD DID NOT CHECK MUST SAY SO … A check that asserts nothing and reports PASS is the V5 tautological-check class". Conflicting: L296 "the probe must never break the audit" (2026-07-27), L318 (2026-06-25), L571 and L857 (2026-06-25: "raw read is best-effort; group B simply skips"), and the module docstring L19 to L23 ("VALIDATE + ALERT only").
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_auditor.py` L266 to L297 (A5), L304 to L319 (A3), L567 to L572 (D's validator call), L579 to L589 (E), L850 to L858 with L334 to L335 (B), L421 to L523 (C), L681 to L710 and L745 to L747 (G); `config_sanity.py` L66 to L80; `main.py` L3459 to L3465.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: A5 and A3 run inside `try/except Exception: pass`; D drops any error of `validate_slippage_overrides`; `_safe_load_yaml` returns `None` on any error, and group B then returns an empty list, which pre-flight renders as a PASS row titled "Single-source integrity" (`worst_in_group` L111 to L115 gives PASS for no findings); E skips a failing getter and reports "no launch-phase params". FACT: groups C, G1 to G3, G5's global window parse and B5's comparison are unguarded. INTERPRETATION: if the import or `strategy_will_trade` (A07) raises during `main.py`'s A5 re-run, no A5 finding is produced, `main.py` sees an empty list, and the boot continues with no log line. At startup the unguarded parts operate on validated values: `TradingHoursConfig`'s own validators parse the same times first (`core/config_loader.py` L109 to L189) and `PositionSizingConfig` and `RiskConfig` bound the percentages, so they cannot fail on a loaded configuration; a `TypeError` or `AttributeError` raised inside the model validator would leave `load_all` unwrapped (synthetic test), and `main.py` would still exit 5 (L2199 to L2201). Whether `strategy_will_trade` can raise on validated strategies is not established (A07). At the file values the probe would find no BLOCK anyway (12 enabled INTRADAY strategies with `trade_type: BOTH`; `strategies/control.py` L67 to L117).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; own strategy-file script; synthetic pydantic test.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; INTERPRETATION. The sources conflict and the code follows "never break the audit". KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given: (a) holds (the handlers); (b) holds (any exception in the probe certainly suppresses the finding); (c) is not shown (no source names an exception in the probe as a case handled under the documented set-up, and whether one can arise is A07's); (d) fails (L296 supports the outcome).
- **Severity or impact where established:** an exception in the probe bypasses the A5 exit-3 gate silently; a failed raw read shows a clean group-B row in the 09:20 report.
- **Owner:** A02; A07 (`strategy_will_trade`), A20 (row rendering), A01 (the gate's call site).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 8; STUDY section 7.3 ("Exception handling").
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; the conflict between `fail_fast_no_silent` and "never break the audit" is for treatment.

##### V-07 — The pre-flight and control-tower audits never check slippage-override symbols, although the guide and the docstring say they do

- **Area and flow:** group D in pre-flight and the control tower.

- **Actual code path:** `audit_app_config` L826 (`known_symbols=None`); `config_sanity.py` L43 to L44; `aggregator.py` L161; `orders/order_placer.py` L308 to L314 (A10).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: neither caller passes `known_symbols`; `validate_slippage_overrides` skips the `by_symbol` typo check when it is `None`; the extreme-value and `by_strategy` checks still run (`known_strategies` comes from the loaded strategies, L837). FACT: `main.py` Phase 3a (L3216 to L3228; A01) does check symbols, but only into the boot log. File values: the three override maps are empty: no effect at the baseline.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; own file-value script.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT.
- **Severity or impact where established:** latent; a typo'd `by_symbol` key would not appear in the 09:20 e-mail or the control tower.
- **Owner:** A02 (the auditor's documented contract); A20 and A15 (the calls).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 8.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### V-08 — What the auditor does not inspect

- **Area and flow:** the coverage of the auditor over the loaded configuration.
- **Issue category:** Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` L547 to L548 (Section 10b, 2026-06-25): an "automatic referee that checks your config makes *sense as a whole* (not just that each value is individually valid)", with "7 rows (A–G)" in the pre-flight report (L553 to L554); the module docstring lists the groups A to G (L25 to L32); the NI-11 comment L468 to L473 (2026-08-23) records that C2 "did not look at" the delivery concentration and position-value keys, so that "this audit stayed silent", and adds C2's delivery arm as "completing the same check, not a new policy" (C2 only). Intended coverage beyond the listed rules: not established.
- **Actual code path:** `audit_app_config` L838 to L839; the group functions L187 to L765.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: only `app_config.system` is passed; `broker_costs`, `broker_limits`, `slippage`, `scoring` (group B reads only the raw `scoring_weights.yaml`), `scan_webhook_map`, `chartink_scanners`, `nse_holidays` and `file_hashes` are not inspected. Of the 42 `SystemConfig` fields it reads 9 (`force_intraday_only`, `trade_type`, `delivery_enabled`, `structure_exit`, `capital`, `position_sizing`, `risk`, `entry_gate`, `trading_hours`); within `trading_hours` no `mis_squareoff_*` key (no such text in the module). No rule ties `eod_squareoff.poll_interval_sec` to `trading_hours.mis_squareoff_margin_sec` (A01 M-24; concern 2, another part). C1, C3, C4 and C5 have no delivery arm (`risk.delivery_daily_loss_limit_pct`, `position_sizing.delivery_risk_per_trade_pct`, `risk.max_open_delivery_positions`), while C2d gives C2 one. G2 checks INTRADAY and COVER_ORDER only (DELIVERY is pinned to 1.0 by the schema, `core/config_loader.py` L317 to L327; BRACKET_ORDER is bounded only by `leverage_safety`). No cross-file rule (for example `scan_webhook_map.yaml` against the strategies, S10, concern 12).
- **Evidence:** `ast` count of `SystemConfig` fields; text search of the module; the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (Weakness: missing validation, as incomplete coverage), bounded to what the code itself shows; not a KNOWN DEFECT. FACT: C1, C3, C4 and C5 check the intraday keys (`risk.daily_loss_limit_pct`, `position_sizing.risk_per_trade_pct`, `position_sizing.max_concentration_pct`, `risk.max_open_positions`) and not their delivery counterparts (`risk.delivery_daily_loss_limit_pct`, `position_sizing.delivery_risk_per_trade_pct`, `position_sizing.delivery_max_concentration_pct`, `risk.max_open_delivery_positions`), which alone govern delivery entries (`core/config_loader.py` L790 to L793) while `delivery_enabled` is true in the file (`config/system_config.yaml` L155): the same sanity check is applied to one book and not to the other. The sources are kept apart: the NI-11 comment records the delivery gap as a bug for C2 alone, now closed by C2d, and does not state that C1, C3, C4 or C5 were meant to have delivery arms; the guide's "as a whole" sets cross-field sense against per-value validity and describes seven rows A to G, so it does not establish coverage of every section. OPEN QUESTION (section 12(b), item 6; Open Issues Register A02-08): whether C1, C3, C4 and C5 were meant to audit the delivery counterparts, and whether the other absences (33 of the 42 `SystemConfig` sections, the seven other sub-configs, the order types outside G2, cross-file rules) were meant to be covered; neither intent is established, and intended symmetry is not inferred from C2d. The ISSUE rests on the asymmetry the code shows, not on a breached requirement; no production consequence is established.
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established; a contradiction among the uninspected sections is not reported by the auditor.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 8; STUDY section 7.3 ("What it does not inspect").
- **Rama:** needed: whether C1, C3 to C5 were meant to audit the delivery counterparts, and which other coverage was intended (Open Issues Register A02-08 (a) and (b)).

##### V-09 — G1 measures the entry window against the end-of-day backstop, not the primary MIS square-off

- **Area and flow:** group G1 (startup, pre-flight, control tower) and the MIS auto-square-off schedule (A12).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** G1 comment L683 "entry window has time to work before square-off" and message L690 to L691 "trades may not have time to hit targets" (2026-06-25); `config/system_config.yaml` L43 to L45 (T5, 29-Jun: `entry_end` aligned to 15:00, which "kills the auditor entry_end-vs-squareoff finding"); L49 to L53 (2026-08-28): `eod_squareoff_time` is "EOD ACCOUNTING + EMERGENCY BACKSTOP -- NOT the primary MIS square-off … Primary MIS safety is mis_squareoff_* below"; L58 "PRIMARY MIS SAFETY".
- **Actual code path:** `_group_g_cross_field` L684 to L692.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: G1 compares `trading_hours.entry_end` with `trading_hours.eod_squareoff_time` and reads no `mis_squareoff_*` key. File values at the baseline: `entry_end` 15:00 and `eod_squareoff_time` 15:17 (17 minutes, no WARN); `mis_squareoff_cutoff` 15:09 with offsets 6m and 3m, so the first MIS pass at 15:03 (comment L90): 3 minutes after `entry_end`, the cutoff 9 minutes after (own arithmetic). HISTORICAL: before `5455ced` (03-Sep-2026, after the last recorded deployment) the values were 15:12, 5m and 2m (first pass 15:07; L60, L92 to L95). The loader requires `entry_end` before the first pass (`core/config_loader.py` L113 to L116) but no minimum gap. INTERPRETATION: since the MIS schedule was added, intraday positions are closed by the MIS passes minutes after the entry window closes, so G1's stated purpose is measured against a time that is not the first to close them, and G1 stays silent.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; own file-value script; `git blame` (L49 to L58: `6f00b4d`, 2026-08-28).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; INTERPRETATION.
- **Severity or impact where established:** at the file values G1 gives no warning while the gap to the first MIS pass is 3 minutes; which values were in force: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A02 (the rule); A12 (the MIS schedule).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 8; STUDY sections 1.8 and 7.3.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; whether G1 should measure to the MIS schedule is a treatment question.

#### 6.3.3 The W0 snapshot

##### V-10 — The W0 snapshot omits the strategy configuration (pending W0.1) and every file outside the registry

- **Area and flow:** `main.py` L2593 to L2614 → `snapshot_config` → `config_snapshots`; readers in A15.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** snapshotter docstring L4 to L12 (2026-07-01): persist "the FULL resolved runtime config" so the report can read "the config AS IT WAS on a given date" and "what config was live on date X"; `main.py` L2594 to L2596 and `core/schema.sql` L1475 to L1479 (same commit). HISTORICAL: `docs/audit/daily_report_redesign_phase0_data_audit_30jun2026.md` L51 lists among the configuration values needing capture "per-strategy params" and L53 records: "DECISION (Rama): (A) add a startup `config_snapshot` DB table that records the resolved SystemConfig (DB-pure), or (B) … Recommended: (A) … ('what config was live on date X')" (a decision posed to Rama in a legacy document; HISTORICAL). `reports/daily_trade_review.py` L1156 (2026-07-01): "STRATEGY — pending W0.1 (per-strategy config is NOT in config_snapshots; strategies load via StrategyLoader)"; `ops_dashboard/backend/readers/config_reader.py` L10 to L11.
- **Actual code path:** `_resolve_config_dict` L107 to L141; `core/config_loader.py` `AppConfig` L2420 to L2434 (no strategies field).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the row holds `AppConfig.model_dump(mode="json")`: the eight registry sub-configs and `file_hashes`. Not captured: the 43 non-registry tracked `config/` files (among them the 16 strategy files and `accounts.csv`) and every environment value (only variable names are in the configuration). The W0.1 step is not implemented at the baseline (no code captures strategy configuration into the table; `git grep`). FACT (A15's reader, call site only): the dashboard's configuration reader parses the strategy files as they are now, not as they were.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git grep` for `W0.1` and `config_snapshots`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (an intended capture not implemented); FACT.
- **Severity or impact where established:** a report re-run for a past date cannot show that date's strategy settings from the table.
- **Owner:** A02 (the snapshotter); A15 (the readers).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 items 10 and 11; STUDY section 7.1.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### V-11 — The W0 duplicate check keys on date and hash only

- **Area and flow:** `snapshot_config` L189 to L211; readers of `config_snapshots` (A15).
- **Issue category:** Flow and state; Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** docstring L19 to L22 (2026-07-01): idempotent per `(snapshot_date, config_hash)`; a differing hash on a same-day restart inserts a row, "capturing the change"; purpose L4 to L7 ("AS IT WAS"); `core/schema.sql` L1475 ("One row per distinct resolved-config seen on a date") and L1481 to L1483; `main.py` L2597 ("mode is a column") and L2599 to L2601.
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_snapshotter.py` L189 to L216; `core/schema.sql` L1491 to L1502.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** MATCH part, FACT: a same-config restart on the same date writes nothing; a new configuration writes a row (tests `tests/unit/test_config_snapshotter.py` L96 to L131, as documents). ISSUE part, FACT: `account_id` and `mode` are not in the key, and the SELECT finds any earlier row with the same date and hash. INTERPRETATION: in a same-day sequence of configuration A, then B, then A again, the third boot writes no row, so the date's latest row stays B while A is loaded; `reports/daily_trade_review.py` reads the date's latest row only (L349 to L351), so the report would show B; a same-day boot with the same configuration under another account or mode writes no row, and the row keeps the first boot's account and mode. FACT: the SELECT and the INSERT are separate steps and the table has no UNIQUE constraint; INTERPRETATION: a duplicate would need two concurrent writers, and the only writer is `main.py`, which runs under the single-instance lock (`main.py` L2183 to L2190; A01), so this is not reachable at the baseline.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git grep` for other writers (none).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; INTERPRETATION (the sequence's consequence); FACT (the key).
- **Severity or impact where established:** the table can name the wrong configuration, account or mode for a date in the sequences above.
- **Owner:** A02; A03 (the table, no UNIQUE); A15 (the readers' latest-row choice; only the dashboard diffs rows).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 10; STUDY section 7.1.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### V-12 — The redaction is a key-substring rule and, on the baseline configuration, a no-op, as intended

- **Area and flow:** `redact_secrets` → `config_json` (durable, in every database backup) and its readers.
- **Issue category:** none (MATCH, reclassified on 08-Nov-2026).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** M-K5 comment L45 to L48 and `redact_secrets` docstring L61 to L73 (`dbeba4a`, 2026-07-16): replace any populated secret-valued field; a no-op in production because the configuration holds variable names; the docstring L23 to L27 (2026-07-01): `config_json` is "secret-free in production" by CL5; LOCKED-YAML CL5 L773 to L775 (values needing environment variables are read by the consumer); Q2 L196 to L198 (secrets in `.env`; the database holds "nothing more sensitive than what's already in .env"); HISTORICAL AUDIT-04JUL L148 (M-K5). For e-mail addresses, IP literals and URLs: intended handling not established.
- **Actual code path:** `core/config_snapshotter.py` L49 to L83, L98 to L99.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (own `ast` and key scans): at the baseline the only value fields whose names contain a marker are `alerts.smtp.password` (`core/config_loader.py` L890, default empty, key absent from the file) and `regime.index_token` (an `int`, L1271); the `*_env` fields hold names and are kept by design (L45 to L48); so the redaction is a no-op on the baseline configuration, as L65 to L69 say (MATCH part). FACT (key paths only, values not reproduced): stored unredacted are three e-mail-shaped values (`alerts.smtp.username`, `alerts.smtp.from_address`, `alerts.smtp.to_addresses[0]`), an IP literal (`webhook.bind_host`), 16 URLs in each of `scan_webhook_map.scanners.*` and `chartink_scanners.scanners.*`, and `fno_ban.url`. INTERPRETATION: the rule would miss a secret under a key without a marker, in a list of strings under a marked key (list items have no key), inside a URL, or as a non-string value. INTERPRETATION (PyYAML): a syntax error near these lines of `system_config.yaml` can quote them into the CRITICAL boot line (`core/config_loader.py` L2496 to L2499; `main.py` L2200).
- **Evidence:** own scans; the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** MATCH (reclassified on 08-Nov-2026 after ChatGPT's review; kept as a block for traceability). FACT: the redaction meets its stated intent, a no-op on a configuration that holds variable names (CL5; L45 to L48, L61 to L73). INTERPRETATION: the latent gaps of the key-substring rule. The storage of e-mail addresses, an IP literal and URLs has no A02 intent source and is recorded as information only, not as an issue (Rama's instruction of 08-Nov-2026, relayed in ChatGPT's review of the same day).
- **Severity or impact where established:** no secret value is stored at the baseline; personal and network data are stored on every boot that writes a row.
- **Owner:** A02; A03 (retention and backups of the table); A15 (readers that display `config_json`).
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 10; STUDY sections 9.5 and 9.6 and question 9.
- **Rama:** not needed (STUDY question 9 is no longer material; AUDIT section 11).
