INTERPRETATION (own re-implementation): with `--apply --confirm` at the baseline the script would set the daily loss limit to 0.05; `gap_fade_long.yaml` has no "TEMP" and is left alone (and reported as already at 60, F-23).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines and commits; own script.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; HISTORICAL; OPEN QUESTION for the procedure (A02-01 (b)). The sources conflict, and the script follows the earlier ones. Four points are kept apart. (i) The intended values, ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE (08-Nov-2026, after ChatGPT's review): the later-dated sources settle 0.03 for `risk.daily_loss_limit_pct` (commit `c69f1fd`, 2026-06-24, declares 3% the sole, permanent daily-loss authority and attributes the resolved conflicts to Rama, HISTORICAL; the file comment L316; the system map calls the 5% a fixture literal, IC-32) and 0 for `gap_fade_long.min_score` (commit `1e41f99`, 2026-06-08; the system map calls the "60" note stale; DEC-04: IC-33). The table's 0.05 and 60, and the tracker's and the guide's, are stale entries; a test fixture of 2026-07-25 still holds 0.05 as the script's clean state, a fixture literal by the system map. (ii) The script's effect: with `--apply --confirm` at the baseline it would set the daily loss limit to 0.05 (INTERPRETATION, own re-implementation); `gap_fade_long.yaml` has no "TEMP" and is left at 0 (F-23). (iii) Whether the guide's revert procedure was still meant to be followed after the June changes: OPEN QUESTION (A02-01 (b)). (iv) Whether the script was ever run: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (F-25). KNOWN DEFECT considered and not given, for the daily-loss case, under the A01 AUDIT's rule (its section 1.6): (a) holds: a value and a branch, the `TEMP_REVERTS` entry (L46 to L54) and `apply_reverts` (L147 to L174); (b) fails: nothing in the language semantics makes the mechanism fail, since the script applies its table value exactly as written; the departure lies in the value itself, which the later sources supersede, a stale value and design concern that the rule leaves as an ISSUE; (c) holds on the documents at the baseline, where the guide's step was never withdrawn, though whether the procedure was still intended is point (iii); (d) holds for the value: setting 0.05 contradicts the later sources that settle 0.03; the superseded entries are not counted as current intent, and the fixture is a test literal. Because (b) fails, no answer to point (iii) can make this a KNOWN DEFECT. For `gap_fade_long` none arises: the script leaves the file at 0, which agrees with the intended value; its misleading report is F-23.
- **Severity or impact where established:** following the deployment guide's step at the baseline would loosen the daily loss limit from 3% to 5% of capital, effective at the next restart (CONFIG_GUIDE L16 to L18).
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 16; STUDY question 15.1.
- **Rama:** needed for one question only (section 12(b), item 1(b); Open Issues Register A02-01 (b)): whether the deployment guide's general TEMP-revert procedure was still meant to be followed at the switch from Paper to Live after the June changes and, if so, whether its scope excluded the daily-loss entry superseded by the 3% authority. The intended daily loss limit (0.03) and `gap_fade_long.min_score` (0) are answered by evidence (result above) and are not put to Rama. Whether the script was ever run: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (F-25).

##### F-23 — The revert report prints the table's values, not the values found in the file

- **Area and flow:** the temporary-configuration mechanism; the dry-run report.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the docstring L9 and L15 ("dry-run, print diff"; "dry-run report printed"); the report's labels "Current:" (L190) and "ALREADY REVERTED" with "-> <value>" (L196 to L198); the tracker L4 and L30 to L31 ("Dry-run (shows what would change)").
- **Actual code path:** `print_report` L177 to L199; `current_line` collected at L128 to L129 and never printed.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: "Current:" prints `temp_value` and "ALREADY REVERTED" prints `prod_value`, whatever the file holds. At the baseline the report would show "Current: 1.00" for a file value of 0.03 and `min_score -> 60` for a file value of 0.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; own re-implementation.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT.
- **Severity or impact where established:** the dry run, the step meant to show what would change, hides the 0.03-to-0.05 change of F-22.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 16.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### F-24 — The revert writes in place without a backup, does not check the value it replaces, and deletes the line's comment

- **Area and flow:** the temporary-configuration mechanism; the write.
- **Issue category:** Failure handling; Weakness.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the deployment guide L239 to L240 (stop the system first, then run the revert); the tracker; the script's safety check (L222 to L224). No source states a backup, an atomic write or a value check; intended behaviour for these is not established.
- **Actual code path:** `apply_reverts` L147 to L174 (re-read per entry L154; replacement through the end of the line L160 to L166; `Path.write_text` in place L169); `main` L231 to L233.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: each entry re-reads and rewrites the file; the replacement runs through the end of the line and replaces any inline comment; no check compares the found value with `temp_value`; no check of the running service, the market hours or the mode. INTERPRETATION: the write is not atomic, and entries written before an exception stay written (exit 1). At the baseline an apply would replace six comments, among them L316's "SOLE daily-loss authority (3% of capital), PERMANENT", with "REVERTED from TEMP" comments.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; own re-implementation.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION for atomicity.
- **Severity or impact where established:** as above; the recovery from a wrong apply is a manual restore.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 16.
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### F-25 — Whether the revert script was ever run, and on which files

- **Area and flow:** the temporary-configuration mechanism.
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/revert_temp_config.py`; the tracked history of `config/system_config.yaml`.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no "REVERTED from TEMP" comment ever entered the tracked history of `config/` (`git log -S`); the TEMP markers were removed by commits (`843d9dc`, 2026-06-07; `1e41f99`, 2026-06-08; `9083db7`, 2026-06-11). UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: whether the script ran on the VM or the PC, before or after 25-Jul-2026, and whether any VM copy of `system_config.yaml` was changed by it (the VM is gone).
- **Evidence:** `git log -S`; the commit messages.
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A02.
- **Cross-reference:** 13.2 item 16; STUDY 13.3 item 5.
- **Rama:** Rama may be able to say whether he ran it; no classification depends on it.

##### F-26 — Which environment each process had, and whether the absent files existed on the VM

- **Area and flow:** environment variables and files expected but absent.
- **Actual code path:** the 57 reads; the three load mechanisms (F-15); the paths of STUDY section 3.5 of the STUDY.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED: the content of the VM and PC `.env` files, of any systemd drop-in, and of the dashboard overlay; whether `config/instruments.csv`, a next-year holiday file, the direction-registry state file and the SSH operator baseline existed on the VM. HISTORICAL measurements exist (LEDGER11 §2: 24 keys on the VM, names only; `docs/audit/audit_05jul2026.md` L170) but are not runtime evidence available to this project.
- **Evidence:** the repository only; the VMs are terminated (Document 06, section 4.2).
- **Result and classification:** UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A02 (names); A17 (the environment files).

## 9. Part K — Configured settings from file to consumer

Inside this part, a reference to "section 1" to "section 7" without a document name means this part's own section 9.1 to 9.7.

### 9.1 Scope and method of this part

- **Scope.** Every configured leaf of `config/system_config.yaml` (42 top-level keys, 327 leaves), `config/broker_limits.yaml` (16 leaves), `config/broker_costs.yaml` (15 leaves) and `config/slippage_model.yaml` (4 leaves), audited from the file through the loader object to each consumer, against the intent stated for it. Not in this part: `security.yaml`, `preflight.yaml` and `expected_managers.yaml` (part F); strategy files (part T); the loader's own validation, defaults and field constraints (part L). Keys absent from the files but read with a loader default (23 in `system_config.yaml`) are named only where they bear on a configured key.
- **Baseline.** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb`; `git rev-parse HEAD` returned it and `git status --short` was empty before the work (06-Nov-2026) and again when the work resumed after an interruption (08-Nov-2026). Every line cited below was re-read at the baseline with `git show 970aabf:<path>` through a helper outside the clone (`work/k/s.sh`), with `git grep -n -w` over the non-test tree for readers, `git log -S` for history and `git blame` for line dates. Nothing in the clone was written, run or imported.
- **Leads.** The A02 STUDY draft (sections 1, 5.4 to 5.6, 10 to 13), the key-to-consumer map parts 1 and 2 and points P5a and P5b. Each lead was re-checked; where a lead and the code differed, the code decided (none of the lead rows used here was found wrong at the cited lines; two leads were refined: the liveness-probe 16:00 bound and the `personal_chat_id_env` key are documented as deliberate at their sites).
- **Intent sources** (all HISTORICAL; dates are the line's `git blame` date unless "file" is written): the YAML comments on and above each key and the file headers (system_config.yaml header L3 to L5, 2026-04-17: "Every numeric and boolean limit here has a corresponding runtime check, Telegram alert, and daily report row (P2)"); the loader field comments and docstrings (`core/config_loader.py`); module docstrings and comments at the consumer; LOCKED-YAML (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml`, file 2026-07-22), in particular P1 L220 to L227, P2 L255 to L261, `principles.config_honor_principle` L424 to L427, G4 L82, G6 L124 to L133, G7 L135 to L141, G8 L148, P12 L333 to L341, P14 L343 to L350, P15 L352 to L358, RL4 L1068 to L1078, CC9 L1243 to L1250, PR3 L1307 to L1318, EOD12 L1557 to L1570, CR10 L1850 to L1860; LOCKED-YAML-0 (`docs/web_claude/00_source_documents/locked_decisions.yaml`, 2026-04-17); FRULES (`docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` 1.4 L31 to L38, file 2026-08-06); CFG-GUIDE (`docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md`, file 2026-07-28; its L7 to L8 say "If a setting here ever disagrees with the actual YAML, the YAML file wins"); M14 (`docs/web_claude/01_module_instructions/module_14_order_monitor.txt`, 2026-04-17); IA-2026 (`docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md`, file 2026-08-01); AUDIT-05JUL (`docs/audit/audit_05jul2026.md`, file 2026-08-07); commit messages (`38fa4e9` 31-May-2026 FIX-133; `03ac3b0` 31-May-2026 FIX-134; `40a37b2` 18-Jun-2026 TASK-10; `afefccb` 25-Jul-2026 M-A2; `dfc4779` 19-Apr-2026 BL-6). Rama has made no statement to this project about A02 behaviour; statements that legacy documents attribute to Rama are quoted as HISTORICAL.
- **Counting.** One ISSUE block per cause (closely related keys grouped). One MATCH row per top-level section of `system_config.yaml` and per broker file (45 rows), giving the number of keys whose configured value reaches every functional consumer as its comment says; a key with an ISSUE part is counted under its ISSUE item, not in the MATCH count. The cross-cutting naming item K-21 does not remove keys from the MATCH counts (each consumer applies the unit its own comment states). A row with no matching key is a coverage row and is not counted as MATCH.
- **Labels.** FACT, INTERPRETATION, HISTORICAL, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, OPEN QUESTION, as in RULES.md. A YAML value is the file value at the baseline, not proof that it was in force. Paper-only paths are marked and not traced. No sensitive value is reproduced (the SMTP addresses, the bind address, the account identifier and home-directory paths are named by key or file only; the F&O ban URL is named, not copied).

### 9.3 Issue and UNKNOWN records

##### K-01 — The `broker` section governs nothing at runtime

- **Area and flow:** broker selection, fallback and multi-account mode; `system_config.yaml` L21 to L25 → `AppConfig.system.broker` (`BrokerConfig`, `core/config_loader.py` L1921 to L1927) → no runtime reader.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** YAML L21 "FIX-133 Item 28: multi-broker support", L24 `fallback_enabled` "enable when AngelOne credentials available", L25 `multi_account_mode` "FIX-133 Item 29: enable only when multi-account tested" (all 2026-05-31, `38fa4e9`); `broker/angelone_adapter.py` L9 to L13 (AO1 "Stub only", AO3 "Enable via system_config.yaml broker.fallback_enabled: true") and L25 to L26 ("Wire this as the fallback adapter in order_placer when broker.fallback_enabled is set to true"), 2026-05-31; commit `38fa4e9` (31-May-2026): "Item 28: broker/angelone_adapter.py stub + BrokerConfig; Item 29: multi-account warning"; the header's P2 (YAML L3 to L4) and LOCKED-YAML P2 / `config_honor_principle` (no "namesake" values). Against: `main.py` L2556 to L2572 (I.4, 2026-04-25) pins "only 'zerodha' is supported in this build"; `core/account_registry.py` L6 and L22 ("multi-account is v2.1"). HISTORICAL: IA-2026 L3727 to L3729 and L3756 (01-Aug-2026) call `multi_account_mode` an orphan "declared for an untested future".
- **Actual code path:** no reader of `system.broker.*` outside the loader and the dashboard (`git grep -w`); the broker is the literal "zerodha" at `broker/product_resolver.py` L95 (default argument), `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L587 and L1586, `main.py` L3293; `main.py` L2561 to L2572 compares the `accounts.csv` broker with "zerodha" (exit 9); `AngelOneAdapter` has no importer; `core/account_registry.py` L196 to L208 warns on more than one enabled account whatever the flag says.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: none of the four values changes behaviour. At the file values (`primary` "zerodha", `fallback_enabled` false, `multi_account_mode` false) the code's fixed state agrees with the file. INTERPRETATION: setting `primary` to another broker or `fallback_enabled` to true would have no effect.
- **Evidence:** `git grep -n -w` for each key name; `git log -S multi_account_mode` (only `38fa4e9`); `git show 38fa4e9` for the account-registry warning; the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT. OPEN QUESTION whether the keys are intended scaffolding (AO1 "stub only") or live switches (YAML wording "enable when ..."). KNOWN DEFECT not given: condition (a) fails (no mechanism consults the keys).
- **Severity or impact where established:** the values never reach a consumer; at the file values no difference arises.
- **Owner:** A02 (the keys); A04 (broker selection, the adapter literals, the stub); A01 (`main.py` L2561 to L2572, L3293); A15 (display, `ops_dashboard/backend/services/config_view.py` L408, L449, L721 to L722).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 18 (`broker.primary`; keys with no consumer); STUDY 5.4.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (the absence conflicts with P2 either way); needed for treatment: whether the section is a scaffold or a switch.

##### K-02 — Trading-hour values held as literals and in the dashboard's own copy

- **Area and flow:** `trading_hours.market_open`, `market_close`, `entry_start`, `entry_end` and `eod_squareoff_time` (YAML L28, L47, L49, L103, L104) reaching consumers outside `MarketWindows`.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components; Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** LOCKED-YAML P1 L222: "All times sourced from core/market_windows.py — single source of truth, no hardcoded times anywhere else. Configurable via config/system_config.yaml"; FRULES 1.4 ("No hardcoded ... settings"); YAML header L5 (2026-04-17); YAML L104 (2026-04-21) `market_close` "cutoff for EOD-window checks"; CFG-GUIDE L199 to L204. Copies documented as deliberate at their sites: `ops_dashboard/backend/config/gui_config.yaml` L61 "Market clock (DUPLICATED from config/system_config.yaml trading_hours)" (2026-07-03); `screening/step_executor.py` L30 to L35 (constant for old tests; production injects the configured open); `scripts/liveness_probe.py` L85 to L100 (25-Jul-2026: the 16:00 bound is "CONSERVATIVE rather than exact ... Do NOT resync this to 17:35"). No later source replaces P1 as a whole.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L994 (`_MARKET_OPEN = _time(9, 15)`, 2026-06-12); `signals/signal_processor.py` L1745 to L1746 (gap window 09:15 to 09:30, 2026-05-30); `ops/control_tower/freshness.py` L18 (`_WINDOW_CLOSE = time(15, 30)`, 2026-06-29); `ops_dashboard/backend/services/execution_analytics.py` L129; `scripts/sr_level_export.py` L158 (literal 15:30, although L147 to L148 read the configured times); `scripts/gemini_watchman.py` L45 to L46 (09:15 and 16:30); `scripts/t2_cnc_gtt_realtest.py` L125; the dashboard clock `ops_dashboard/backend/services/freshness.py` L43 to L93 and `capacity.py` L209 to L217, which read `gui_config.yaml` L63 to L69; `freshness.in_entry_window` L68 runs from `entry_start` to `eod_squareoff`.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: these consumers never read `trading_hours`. At the file values the copies equal the configured times, except the watchman's 16:30 close and the dashboard's entry window, which ends at 15:17, not at `entry_end` 15:00. INTERPRETATION: an edit of `trading_hours`, or a special session, would not reach them. FACT (the MATCH part): `MarketWindows` (`main.py` L2320 to L2329), `EodSquareoff` (L3312), `TokenMonitor` (L3396 to L3397), the boot check (L2228 to L2232), the MIS timing (L3329 to L3337), the MIS notifier (`alerts/mis_squareoff_notifier.py` L164 to L172) and the self-exit thread (L2211, L4142) read the configured values.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git grep` for 09:15 and 15:30 literals (the sites listed in the key map, each re-read; not an exhaustive literal search).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT (copies and literals against P1 and FRULES 1.4; three of them deliberate by their own comments, so sources conflict). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) fails (no code consults the keys at these sites).
- **Severity or impact where established:** at the file values no value differs except the two noted; any edit of `trading_hours` leaves these consumers on the old times.
- **Owner:** A02 (the keys); A01 (`main.py` L994); A06 (gap window); A15 (dashboard, control tower, watchman, execution analytics, liveness probe); A08 (`sr_level_export.py`); A19 (t2 script).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 18 ("the dashboard's own copy of the trading hours"); STUDY 5.5; A01 C11 and C23 (they record `main.py` L994 and `step_executor.py` L36; A02 adds the configured keys' part and the other sites); A01 C35 (special sessions).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify.

##### K-03 — `order_monitor.poll_interval_sec`: two sources state different cadences

- **Area and flow:** `order_monitor.poll_interval_sec` (YAML L193, value 2) → `main.py` L3003 → `broker/order_monitor.py` L174, L211.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** LOCKED-YAML P14 L343 to L350 (and LOCKED-YAML-0 L317 to L320, 2026-04-17): "order_monitor poll interval: 15s (down from 30s). order_reconciler poll interval: 15s ... Both configurable in config/system_config.yaml". Against: M14 OM2 L16 to L25 (2026-04-17) `poll_interval_sec=2, # from system_config.yaml` and OM14 L108 to L111 ("default 2, validated >= 1"); YAML L192 to L193 "OM14 ... poll broker every 2s for fill updates (>= 1)" (2026-04-17); CFG-GUIDE L225 to L231 ("2 / 60"). P14 names `orders/order_monitor.py`; the module is `broker/order_monitor.py`.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3003; `OrderMonitor.__init__` L174 (default 2), L211.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: 2 seconds reaches the monitor; the file value has been 2 since `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026). The reconciler's 15 seconds (YAML L469) agrees with P14.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git log -S'poll_interval_sec: 2'`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; OPEN QUESTION (two same-date sources, neither naming the other). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (d) fails (M14 supports the outcome).
- **Severity or impact where established:** not established (the effect of 2 seconds against 15 on the broker request budget is A04's).
- **Owner:** A02 (the key); A04 (the monitor).
- **Cross-reference:** none in the STUDY concerns; found by this audit's intent check.
- **Rama:** needed, to say which source states the intended cadence.

##### K-04 — `excluded_symbols` is read two ways

- **Area and flow:** `excluded_symbols` (YAML L131) → the webhook receiver (through the model) and the daily report (raw YAML).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** YAML L130 (2026-05-17) "FIX-C: Symbol exclusion list (rejected at webhook edge with DEBUG log only)"; CFG-GUIDE L365 to L370 ("Add a symbol here to stop trading it entirely").
- **Actual code path:** `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L708 to L717 (after alias resolution, upper-case comparison); `reports/daily_report.py` `load_report_data` L140 to L161 (direct `yaml.safe_load`, errors swallowed at L160 to L161, default `[]`), L481 and L695 (case-sensitive membership).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the receiver rejects case-insensitively after alias resolution; the report counts stored signals whose symbol matches an entry exactly. INTERPRETATION: a lower-case entry or an alias name would be rejected by the receiver and not counted by the report; at the file values (five upper-case symbols) the rules agree for upper-case stored symbols. Whether rejected excluded symbols are stored as signals is A06's and A15's.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT (two readers, two matching rules); INTERPRETATION for the effect. Not a KNOWN DEFECT (no case contradicts every source at the file values).
- **Severity or impact where established:** report figures only; the trading gate is the receiver's.
- **Owner:** A02 (the key); A06 (receiver); A15 (report).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 5.5; STUDY 13.2 item 13 (direct readers; other part).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### K-05 — The three delivery-gate flags are read with different predicates and defaults

- **Area and flow:** `force_intraday_only` (YAML L142), `trade_type` (L149), `delivery_enabled` (L155) → the main process, the Cron Officer's strategy-status table, the dashboard and the constructors.
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** YAML L138 to L141 (MIS-only safety; "Set false only when positional/DELIVERY trading is deliberately re-enabled"), L144 to L148 (`trade_type` gates only), L151 to L154 (a real CNC order "ONLY when delivery_enabled=true AND force_intraday_only=false AND trade_type in {DELIVERY,BOTH} ... Enforced at the broker boundary"); value lines 2026-08-04; YAML L273 to L280 (NI-3, 22-Aug-2026): "THE FACT TODAY: force_intraday_only is FALSE ..., delivery_enabled is TRUE ..., trade_type is BOTH ..., and delivery has traded"; CFG-GUIDE L637 to L640: the status table is "computed by the exact same logic the live entry gate uses, so the table never lies". Recorded conflict, replaced: CFG-GUIDE L52, L217 to L223, L632 to L646 (force_intraday_only "true ... leave ON"; "keep trade_type: INTRADAY") and `main.py` L2689 to L2694 ("force_intraday_only=true (current state)", 2026-06-26) are superseded by the later YAML L273 to L280, which states the current values.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L2695 to L2701 (the three-flag predicate for the bucket split), L2406 and L2410 (adapter), L3620 to L3621 (`SignalProcessor`, without `delivery_enabled`); `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L593 (the CNC lock tests `delivery_enabled` only); `scripts/cron_officer.py` L471 to L476 (raw YAML; defaults "INTRADAY" and True; no `delivery_enabled`); `ops_dashboard/backend/services/controls.py` L229 to L232 (`delivery_on` without `delivery_enabled`; default True), `operations.py` L37 to L43, `capacity.py` L128; constructor defaults `ZerodhaAdapter` L391 and L397 (False), `SignalProcessor` L193 to L194, `StrategyLoader` L106; loader defaults L2030 (True), L2037 (False), L2044 ("INTRADAY").
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the main process passes the configured values everywhere. The status table and the dashboard compute "delivery on" or "will trade" without `delivery_enabled`, and the raw readers and constructors carry defaults that differ from each other. INTERPRETATION: with `delivery_enabled` false and the other two allowing delivery, the table and the dashboard would show delivery as on while the adapter refuses every CNC order; at the file values (false, BOTH, true) all readers agree.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT (different predicates and defaults); INTERPRETATION for the effect. A02's part (the key that does not reach the table and the dashboard) is not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) fails for the key. Whether the status table's "never lies" claim meets the KNOWN DEFECT conditions in its own code is for A16 (and the dashboard's for A15) to apply.
- **Severity or impact where established:** display and briefing only at the file values; no trading-path difference.
- **Owner:** A02 (keys and defaults); A16 (`cron_officer.py`, the status rows); A15 (dashboard); A04 (adapter lock); A06 and A07 (strategy control).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 5.5; STUDY 13.2 items 4 (loader defaults, part L), 12 (removed rewrite messages, part T) and 13 (direct readers' defaults, other part).
- **Rama:** not needed.

##### K-07 — `emergency_exit_buffer_pct` and `sl_limit_offset_pct` bypassed by constants

- **Area and flow:** `capital.emergency_exit_buffer_pct` (YAML L206) and `capital.sl_limit_offset_pct` (L204) → configured consumers, and four sites that use `orders/price_math.py` constants instead.
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** YAML L206 (2026-06-16, FIX-181) "emergency/kill exits use marketable LIMIT (LTP ± 1%)"; YAML L204 (2026-06-15) stop-limit offset; loader L346, L351; `orders/price_math.py` L119 to L124 (2026-06-16): `EMERGENCY_EXIT_BUFFER_PCT` "Overridable via capital.emergency_exit_buffer_pct"; `price_math.py` L156 to L159 (2026-06-15): `DEFAULT_SL_LIMIT_OFFSET_PCT` "Mirrors config capital.sl_limit_offset_pct; kept here so the pure helper has a sane fallback when a caller has no config wired (tests, recovery paths)"; P2; FRULES 1.4. HISTORICAL: AUDIT-05JUL L589 ("a config change silently desyncs paths"), L598, L609 ("unify emergency-buffer constant to the config value"); IA-2026 IA-XDUP-01 L3601 to L3619 (01-Aug-2026: two sources that "agree only by value coincidence").
- **Actual code path:** buffer: configured at `KillSwitch` (`main.py` L2250) and `OrderPlacer` (L3084); constant at `orders/eod_squareoff.py` L1542 to L1544 (2026-06-16), `orders/order_reconciler.py` L2323 to L2325 (2026-06-16), `orders/structure_exit_manager.py` L438 (2026-06-27), `scripts/t2_cnc_gtt_realtest.py` L265, L275. Offset: configured at `LimitTripleProtocol` (L3021), `CncGttPlacer` (L3030, re-used as the GTT target offset, documented at L3024), `StructureExitManager` (L3829 to L3830); constant at `orders/order_reconciler.py` L3297 and L3314 (crash-recovery SL, 2026-06-15).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the EOD residual exit, the reconciler flatten, the structure-exit flatten and the crash-recovery SL use the constants; at the file values they are equal (0.01 and 0.005). INTERPRETATION: an edited key would change the kill-switch and placer exits but not those four paths.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git blame` dates.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT. Sources conflict for the SL offset (`price_math.py` names recovery paths as intended users of the constant; the YAML, P2 and FRULES 1.4 make the key the source). For the buffer, `price_math.py` L123 says the constant is overridable by the key, which these paths do not do. Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) fails (no code on those paths consults the key), and for the offset condition (d) fails.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the file values; a tuned value would diverge silently between kill and EOD or recovery exits.
- **Owner:** A02 (the keys); A12 (EOD, reconciler); A11 (structure exit); A19 (t2 script); A13 and A10 (configured consumers).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 18; P5a item 3.
- **Rama:** needed for the SL offset only: whether the recovery path's fixed constant is intended.

- **Actual code path:** fractions: the `capital`, `position_sizing` and `risk` `*_pct` keys, `signal_queue.backpressure_pct` and `warning_pct`, `signal_processor.tgt_min_pct`, `order_reconciler.capital_drift_tolerance_pct` (`orders/order_reconciler.py` L3876 to L3878), `eod_squareoff.limit_aggressive_pct` (`orders/eod_squareoff.py` L1280, L1284), `v3_chain.pullback_proximity_pct` (`screening/hard_gate.py` L449), `watchlist.gap_guard_pct` (`v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L188), `smart_tgt.trigger_pct` and `step_pct`. Percentages: `sr_detector.cluster_pct` (`sr_detector/zones.py` L54), `merge_pct`, `band_buffer_pct`, `entry_proximity_pct`, `near_zone_buffer_pct`, `retest_max_away_pct`, `breakout_margin_pct`, `sl_buffer_pct` (`signals/signal_processor.py` L2438, L2441), `structure_exit.sl_buffer_pct` (`orders/structure_exit_manager.py` L526 to L528) and `break_buffer_pct`, `entry_gate.max_entry_slippage_pct` (`orders/order_placer.py` L1124), `entry_gate.max_spread_pct` (L4097 to L4098), all `broker_costs` rates.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: each consumer applies the unit its own comment states; the suffix does not tell the unit; only the MIS `_percent` keys are named for their unit and range-guarded (loader L709 to L731). INTERPRETATION: about fifteen percentage-valued `_pct` keys contradict the convention stated at L366 to L371; a value entered in the other unit passes the loader for most keys (no range checks, STUDY 13.2 item 7).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT (names against a stated convention); Weakness. Not a KNOWN DEFECT.
- **Severity or impact where established:** none at the file values.
- **Owner:** A02 (key names); the consumers' owners for unit handling (A08, A11, A10, A12, A18, A04).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 19 (`_pct` suffix); STUDY 10.2; the strategy schema's `*_pct` units are part T's.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; needed for treatment (which convention is meant file-wide).

##### K-22 — The SL-buffer keys: a misnamed path and an untied duplicate

- **Area and flow:** `sr_detector.sl_buffer_pct` (YAML L540, 0.2), the loader field `sr_detector.retest_sl_buffer_pct` (L1182, default 0.3, absent from the file) and `structure_exit.sl_buffer_pct` (YAML L635, 0.2).
- **Issue category:** Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** YAML L540 (2026-06-27) "structure SL beyond the broken level by this %"; loader L1211 ("structure SL = band_low − this%"); loader L1182 (2026-06-27, no comment); YAML L635 (2026-06-27) "SL-to-structure buffer ... — match Phase A".
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3610 (`retest_sl_buffer_pct=_sr_cfg.sl_buffer_pct`) → `signals/signal_processor.py` L2438 to L2441; `screening/retest_monitor.py` L297; `sr_detector/zone_builder.py` L74 (`retest_sl_buffer_pct`, default 0.3) → `sr_detector/flags.py` L180 to L185; `orders/structure_exit_manager.py` L131, L526 to L528.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the signal processor's parameter named `retest_sl_buffer_pct` receives `sr_detector.sl_buffer_pct`, while the field of that name feeds the V1 flags' proposed retest SL; so the shadow proposal uses 0.3 % and the Phase A retest 0.2 %. `structure_exit.sl_buffer_pct` equals Phase A's value by hand only. INTERPRETATION: an edit of `sr_detector.sl_buffer_pct` would not move the V1 proposal or the structure-exit buffer.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT. Not a KNOWN DEFECT.
- **Severity or impact where established:** shadow and dormant paths at the file values (`wait_for_retest_enabled` and `structure_exit_enabled` are false).
- **Owner:** A02; A08 (detector, flags, retest); A06 (signal processor); A11 (structure exit).

##### K-24 — Cross-section reuse, and a lookback key that does not set the fetch its comment names

- **Area and flow:** `watchlist.capture_fetch_lookback_days` (YAML L624), `v3_chain.fetch_lookback_days` (L614) and `sr_detector.cache_ttl_sec` (L512).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual; Consistency across components.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** YAML L624 and loader L1539 (2026-07-13): "how far back to fetch daily candles to compute LEVEL (calendar days)"; YAML L512 (2026-06-27) "intra-session (symbol, interval) re-fetch suppression" (sr_detector's own fetches); YAML L612 and L615 (2026-07-12): the V3 worker "reuses the sr_detector rate-limited closure" with its "own OhlcFetcher cache TTL (distinct from sr_detector.cache_ttl_sec)"; `main.py` L3725 to L3727 ("ONE fetcher over the shared closure: lookback covers the structural fetch; cache_ttl=0 keeps the entry stage's 5-min poll FRESH").
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` L3728 to L3732 (the watchlist fetcher's lookback from `v3_chain.fetch_lookback_days`; TTL literal 0.0); `v3_chain/watchlist_capture.py` L154 and L192 to L194 (`fetch_timeframes`, no lookback) → `sr_detector/fetch.py` L123; `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L289 to L291 (`capture_fetch_lookback_days` for the baseline volume only); `main.py` L3519 to L3523 (the regime fetcher's TTL from `sr_detector.cache_ttl_sec`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the EOD LEVEL capture fetches 180 days (`v3_chain.fetch_lookback_days`), not 60; `capture_fetch_lookback_days` sets the entry stage's baseline-volume fetch; the regime engine's cache TTL follows the `sr_detector` key. INTERPRETATION: LEVEL uses the last 20 sessions (`level_lookback_sessions`), so 180 against 60 does not change LEVEL while both cover 20 sessions; an edit of `capture_fetch_lookback_days` does not change the capture.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; FACT (the key against its own comment); the regime TTL reuse has no stated intent (INTERPRETATION). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) fails.
- **Severity or impact where established:** shadow paths; none on LEVEL at the file values.
- **Owner:** A02; A18 (watchlist, regime); A08 (fetcher).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 19 (lookbacks and TTLs across sections); P5b items 5 and 16.
- **Rama:** not needed.

### 9.6 Totals

- **Items:** 79 (34 ISSUE blocks, 45 MATCH-table rows).
- **MATCH:** 36 rows (the other 9 rows are coverage rows: 8 with no matching key, 1 paper-only).
- **ISSUE:** 34 (K-01 to K-34).
- **UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED:** 0 items (UNKNOWN components are noted inside K-19, K-20, K-29 and in section 7).
- **KNOWN DEFECT within ISSUE:** 0 for A02's parts. Considered and not given in every block (mostly condition (a): no mechanism consults the key; for K-03, K-07, K-15, K-16, K-20 and K-34 condition (d): a source supports the outcome; K-11 condition (b); K-12 condition (c)). K-17's dashboard read and K-05's status-table claim are left to A15 and A16 to test.
- **Keys covered:** 362 leaves (327 + 16 + 15 + 4): 261 matching (system_config 240, broker_limits 13, broker_costs 8), 96 in ISSUE items (system_config 82, broker_limits 3, broker_costs 7, slippage_model 4), 5 paper-only (`paper` 4, `telegram_alerts_in_paper_mode` 1).
- **Classification of the 34 ISSUEs:** FACT 32 (K-23 included, its basis restated on 08-Nov-2026; K-01 with an OPEN QUESTION on intent; K-04, K-05, K-11, K-16, K-27 with INTERPRETATION for the effect); INTERPRETATION 1 (K-12); OPEN QUESTION 1 (K-03). Sources conflict in K-02, K-07, K-15, K-20, K-21 and K-34 (classified FACT with the conflict recorded) and in K-03.
- **Items needing Rama:** 6 (K-01 treatment; K-03; K-07 SL-offset part; K-15 treatment; K-21 treatment; K-34).

### 9.7 Could not establish

- Which file values, environment variables and working directories were in force on either VM; whether any key was edited there (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; Document 06, section 4.2).
- Whether the relative sentinel paths (K-20) and the relative `config` directory of the factories (K-16) resolved to the repository root in every process: depends on the declared units and cron lines (A16, A17) and on what ran.
- Library behaviour outside the repository: KiteTicker's initial reconnect delay (K-30) and kiteconnect's default timeout for clients built without one (K-32).
- Whether a soft kill on sustained HTTP 429 exists anywhere outside the three files searched (K-32; A04).
- Whether the liquidity check's absence (K-29) or the missing per-signal deadline (K-13) affected any trade; whether the e-mail fallback variables were set (K-19).
- Whether the dashboard ever displayed the alert status (K-17) or the controls screen's score label (K-27) to the operator.
- Which of P14 (15 s) or M14 (2 s) was meant for the fill monitor (K-03): OPEN QUESTION for Rama.

### 9.8 Item added at assembly

Parts F and K each assigned one point to the other (part F, section 8.2, concern 14; part K, row K-50), so that neither recorded it. Claude checked it at assembly against the baseline source (`config/system_config.yaml` L327 to L342 with `git blame`; `main.py` L3992 to L3993; `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L171 to L176; `git log -1 d922007`) and records it here, so that it is audited once; the independent check of 08-Nov-2026 re-checked it (section 14).

##### X-01 — The `webhook.bind_host` comment still rests the all-interfaces bind on `require_hmac=true`, which the file sets false

- **Area and flow:** the webhook server settings (`config/system_config.yaml` `webhook`, L327 to L342) to the webhook receiver (A06).
- **Issue category:** Intended against actual (a comment against the value it relies on).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `config/system_config.yaml` L328 to L334 (`2d1e580`, 2026-04-27): bind all interfaces so that the VM can accept the scanner webhooks; loopback is "the safer default"; "the all-interfaces bind here relies on require_hmac=true plus firewall/security-group restrictions". L336 (`d922007`, 2026-07-02, C-2): `require_hmac: false`, "Chartink cannot sign payloads; use ?token= auth (WEBHOOK_SECRET required in BOTH modes as of C-2 02-Jul)". The later source sets the value and names the replacement mechanism; it does not mention the bind comment.
- **Actual code path:** `config/system_config.yaml` L328 (`bind_host`, value not reproduced: an all-interfaces address) and L336 (`require_hmac`); `main.py` L3992 to L3993 binds the server to `bind_host` and `bind_port`; `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L171 to L176 reads `require_hmac` from the configuration (A06; row K-50).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: at the file values the receiver binds all interfaces with `require_hmac` false, and the bind comment still names `require_hmac=true` as the premise of that bind. The value follows the later-dated source (MATCH part); the comment's premise is stale (ISSUE part). How the token check constrained the receiver is A06's.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git blame -L 328,336` at the baseline; `git log -1 d922007` ("security(C-2): webhook paper-parity secret + per-IP rate limit"); part K row K-50.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE / FACT. KNOWN DEFECT not considered: condition (a) fails (a comment is no mechanism).
- **Severity or impact where established:** documentation only; the deployment's network restrictions are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A02 (the comment and the keys); A06 (the receiver's token check).
- **Cross-reference:** STUDY 13.2 item 14; STUDY question 4; row K-50.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; STUDY question 4 (section 11) asks whether the all-interfaces bind was still meant to rest on the token check alone.

## 12. Findings for discussion with Rama

Every finding is for the discussion with Rama after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5); none is decided here.

(a) Established by evidence: the KNOWN DEFECTs first, then the other ISSUE items in part order (their classifications are in each record; an item marked "also in (b)" needs Rama's intent as well):

- T-01: KNOWN DEFECT (provisional). `validate_strategy` does not raise `ConfigSchemaError` on every failure (S8).
- F-20: KNOWN DEFECT (provisional). The revert script's "TEMP" test is a substring test: it matches the word "ATTEMPT" and the comment the script itself writes.
- L-01: The holiday file: one year, fixed at import from the host-local date, and required by every load.
- L-02: The MIS timing check at load uses the literal poll interval 5.
- L-03: `special_sessions`: type only, and the documented example fails the load.
- L-04: "No defaults, all required" against 289 code defaults.
- L-05: CL6's split rule is not applied.
- L-06: The MIS timing defaults are the values the 03-Sep change replaced.
- L-07: Which class a missing key and a YAML parse error give.
- L-08: Failures that leave `load_all` outside the two documented classes.
- L-09: Duplicate keys in a YAML mapping are accepted, the last value winning.
- L-10: Lax type coercion against "NO coercion" and two type guards.
- L-11: NaN and infinity pass validators whose messages state a range.
- L-12: Constraints stated in comments, decisions or instructions that the schema does not enforce.
- L-13: Defaults and field sets stated in instructions and decisions that the schema does not follow.
- L-14: Mapping-typed fields accept any keys, outside `extra="forbid"`.
- L-15: Credential-name fields accept any string, including an empty one.
- L-16: The docstring calls the module the loader for all non-strategy YAML files.
- L-17: Validator messages that describe a stricter format than the code checks.
- V-01: The key-usage validator is never fed: every key is reported unaccessed and drift is never detected.
- V-02: The validator's walk registers less than "ALL config values".
- V-03: The validator's docstrings describe a method, a gate and a usage that the code does not have.
- V-04: The auditor's descriptions of its callers, startup groups, logging and context differ from the code.
- V-05: B5 is a BLOCK in a group documented as WARN-only, and it can fire only on raw values that the schema has already accepted.
- V-06: The auditor swallows errors in its probes, so the A5 boot gate can pass silently, and a skipped group B is shown as PASS.
- V-07: The pre-flight and control-tower audits never check slippage-override symbols, although the guide and the docstring say they do.
- V-08: What the auditor does not inspect (also in (b)).
- V-09: G1 measures the entry window against the end-of-day backstop, not the primary MIS square-off.
- V-10: The W0 snapshot omits the strategy configuration (pending W0.1) and every file outside the registry.
- V-11: The W0 duplicate check keys on date and hash only.
- V-13: The snapshotter logs the broker account identifier, though its docstring says only the row id, the hash prefix and the decision are logged.
- T-02: The S10 cross-check is never requested, while the map's header and the decisions say it runs at startup.
- T-03: A missing or empty strategy directory blocks the boot without the invalid-strategy alert.
- T-04: A duplicate `name` silently replaces an earlier strategy; `name` is not tied to the file name.
- T-05: S9 "Strategies loaded ONCE at startup" against repeated loads.
- T-06: Value constraints: the S4 percentage rule and the type-only fields.
- T-07: The `*_pct` unit convention differs inside the schema.
- T-08: S5 ("every field declared in every YAML") is neither met nor enforced (also in (b)).
- T-09: S6 (positional strategies) against the three positional files (also in (b)).
- T-10: S13 (per-category defaults) against the intraday files (also in (b)).
- T-11: Entry windows: the file values, their own comments and the other sources disagree (also in (b)).
- T-12: The schema accepts ATR methods that the consumer does not implement.
- T-13: Texts still describe the removed load-time DELIVERY-to-INTRADAY rewrite.
- T-14: Strategy fields that are configured and that nothing reads (A02's part of the A07 handoff).
- T-15: Three raw readers and one text scanner read the strategy files without the schema.
- T-16: `_describe_config_error` is documented as "path-free" but returns the file path for three failure kinds.
- T-17: S11: the loader imports pydantic, which LOCKED-YAML S11 and its own docstring do not list.
- T-18: The strategy guide describes a schema and values that the code no longer has.
- T-19: `strategy_direction_registry.yaml`: the header says "auto-maintained", while production never writes it.
- T-20: `scoring_weights.yaml` is "the single source", while copies and fallbacks exist elsewhere.
- F-01: `slippage_model.yaml`'s header names consumers that do not exist and a use the code does not make.
- F-03: `chartink_scanners.yaml`: a required file whose values cannot reach the check it is said to serve, with conflicting statements about whether its URLs are placeholders.
- F-04: `expected_managers.yaml`: a stale header, and a file the live boot needs in effect although no list declares it.
- F-06: `security.yaml`'s header says "Alert-ONLY. Nothing here blocks access", while the same file's copy-protection block is a blocking control.
- F-07: `security.yaml` is read without validation, by readers that treat a missing or misspelt key differently.
- F-08: `preflight.yaml`: "Editing this file CANNOT break the boot" does not hold for every edit, and its one value is not validated.
- F-09: A comment tells the operator to use a "non-committed config_overrides file", which no code supports.
- F-10: The plaintext SMTP password field is accepted in every mode, although its comment limits it to tests and development.
- F-11: `main.py --config` overrides the configuration directory only for some readers.
- F-12: Four hand-kept lists of required configuration files.
- F-13: A01 C12, the environment side: credential names are configured in one place, listed in three, and required differently.
- F-14: `.env.example`, the template for `.env`, omits 20 names that the code reads, among them names that scheduled processes require.
- F-16: Credential names come from two sources, and the configured source has no column for the broker user identifier and password.
- F-17: The template calls one account block "ENABLED" while `accounts.csv` disables that account.
- F-21: The revert patterns are unanchored and blind to sections.
- F-22: The revert table's "production" values differ from the file values and from the later sources (also in (b)).
- F-23: The revert report prints the table's values, not the values found in the file.
- F-24: The revert writes in place without a backup, does not check the value it replaces, and deletes the line's comment.
- K-01: The `broker` section governs nothing at runtime.
- K-02: Trading-hour values held as literals and in the dashboard's own copy.
- K-03: `order_monitor.poll_interval_sec`: two sources state different cadences (also in (b)).
- K-04: `excluded_symbols` is read two ways.
- K-05: The three delivery-gate flags are read with different predicates and defaults.
- K-06: Broker product codes compared as literals beside `product_map`.
- K-07: `emergency_exit_buffer_pct` and `sl_limit_offset_pct` bypassed by constants (also in (b)).
- K-08: Four keys validated or configured but not passed: the constructor default applies.
- K-09: `dynamic_by_winrate`, `min_multiplier` and `max_multiplier` have no behavioural effect.
- K-10: `capital.leverage_safety.max_allowed` and the auditor's literal 10.
- K-11: Pre-flight's signal checks hold their own port and backpressure threshold.
- K-12: Consumer-side floors and fallbacks replace values that the loader accepts.
- K-13: `signal_processor.pipeline_timeout_sec` has no consumer.
- K-14: `eod_squareoff.auto_resume_kill_switch` has no consumer.
- K-15: 33 notifier call sites ignore the YAML alert settings.
- K-16: `alerts.telegram.enabled` is read two ways, with opposite failure policies.
- K-17: The dashboard reads `alerts.telegram_enabled`, which does not exist.
- K-18: `alerts.telegram.whitelist_only` has no consumer.
- K-19: SMTP host, port and TLS held twice, with different meanings.
- K-20: `alerts.sentinel_dir` bypassed by literals.
- K-21: The `_pct` suffix means a fraction in some keys and a percentage in others.
- K-22: The SL-buffer keys: a misnamed path and an untied duplicate.
- K-23: `sr_detector.max_queue` does not govern the ZoneWarmer's queue.
- K-24: Cross-section reuse, and a lookback key that does not set the fetch its comment names.
- K-25: `regime.index_symbol` is stored and never read.
- K-26: `watchlist.entry_tf` (replaced by a literal) and `watchlist.playbook_scanner` (no reader).
- K-27: The `v3_chain` thresholds share names and values with `scoring_weights.yaml`, and the dashboard presents one as the eligibility bar.
- K-28: `smart_tgt.enabled`: the required master switch reaches no acting consumer.
- K-29: The entry-gate liquidity check is configured on and never runs.
- K-30: The three `live_feed` keys are not passed to `LiveFeedManager`.
- K-31: `fno_ban`: three copies of the URL, and a load failure silently drops `fail_closed`.
- K-32: `broker_limits.yaml`: the 429 sequence and the connect timeout are never read; the read timeout reaches one client.
- K-33: Broker cost rates repeated as literal defaults in the daily report's own computation.
- K-34: `slippage_model.yaml` has no Live consumer, and its sources disagree on its role (also in (b)).
- X-01: The `webhook.bind_host` comment still rests the all-interfaces bind on `require_hmac=true`, which the file sets false.

(b) Needing Rama's intent or decision (entered in the Open Issues Register; ten findings in ten entries and eleven questions for Rama: A02-01 has three subtopics, of which two are answered by evidence and one is for Rama, and A02-08 has two questions; the register's IDs A02-06 and A02-07 were withdrawn on 08-Nov-2026 and are not reused):

1. A02-01, F-22 (STUDY question 1; IC-32, IC-33): three subtopics, one question for Rama:
   - (a) the daily loss limit: ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE, 0.03 (commit `c69f1fd`, 24-Jun-2026; the file comment L316); the table's 0.05 is a stale entry; not put to Rama;
   - (b) for Rama: whether the deployment guide's general TEMP-revert procedure was still meant to be followed at the switch from Paper to Live after the June changes and, if so, whether its scope excluded the daily-loss entry superseded by the 3% authority (whether the script was ever run is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, F-25);
   - (c) `gap_fade_long.min_score`: ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE, 0 (commit `1e41f99`, 08-Jun-2026, and the later sources, IC-33); the table's 60 is a stale entry; not put to Rama.
2. A02-02, T-08 (STUDY question 6): whether locked decision S5 ("every field declared in every YAML") still governs the strategy files.
3. A02-03, T-09 (STUDY question 6): whether S6's ATR methods remain the intent for the three positional strategies.
4. A02-04, T-10 (STUDY question 6): which gap_go `sl_pct` was intended, 0.01 or 0.012.
5. A02-05, T-11 (STUDY question 6): whether the uniform 15:00 entry end was meant to be temporary, and which entry windows were intended for Live.
6. A02-08, V-08, two questions: (a) whether C1, C3, C4 and C5 were meant to audit the delivery counterparts of the intraday keys they check (that they do not is established; C2 has its delivery arm, C2d); (b) which coverage beyond the listed rules was intended (the auditor reads 9 of the 42 `SystemConfig` sections and none of the other seven sub-configs).
7. A02-09, V-14: whether change detection was meant to cover the strategy files and `accounts.csv`.
8. A02-10, K-03: which source states the intended fill-monitor cadence: P14 (15 s) or the file and M14 (2 s).
9. A02-11, K-07 (the SL-offset part): whether the recovery path's fixed SL-limit offset constant is intended.
10. A02-12, K-34: whether the slippage model was meant to enter Live cost calculations.

## 14. Coverage gaps

- Runtime: which files, values and environment were in force on either VM, which commit production ran after `7d4970a`, and what the validator, the auditor, the snapshot table and the CONFIG_DIFF path recorded, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (L-18, V-15, V-16, T-21, F-15, F-25, F-26). HISTORICAL: a VM-only edit of `min_pass_score` is recorded (section 3).
- Library behaviour was tested on synthetic inputs at pydantic 2.13.5 (pinned 2.13.0), PyYAML 6.0.3 and python-dotenv 1.2.3 (pinned 1.2.2); the installed versions on the VMs are unknown. Type conformance of the files to the models was checked by key and required field, not by importing the legacy models.
- Consumers' internals were not traced; where a part's finding depends on them (for example `strategy_will_trade` in V-06, the `trailing_sl_*` guard and the order protocol in T-14, the dashboard's reads in K-17), the open point is handed to the owner as an OPEN QUESTION or a test for that topic.
- Sources not in the repository: SPEC section 8 on configuration files, a separate document of the twelve BUILD 1 conflicts, and the assistant memory notes cited by the configuration guide (evidence file, section 7). The long registers and audit documents were read where the search hit, not end to end.
- The parts' own lists of what they could not establish are in sections 5.7, 6.7, 7.7, 8.7 and 9.7.
- Assembly checks: Claude re-read the code lines of the two KNOWN DEFECT records (`strategies/schema.py` L350 to L375; `scripts/revert_temp_config.py` L116 to L168) and of X-01, and checked the merges against their records. After ChatGPT's review of 08-Nov-2026, a separate helper that had written none of the parts checked, against the code and the index: every corrected record (V-12, V-14, F-18, F-19, K-23, X-01, the KNOWN DEFECT records), every record whose result or count changed, the ISSUE records whose intent is not established (that each names a defect or weakness the code itself demonstrates), every cross-topic handoff, the count tables and the register entries with their line references. Its eight points and two borderline records are corrected (section 1.5); F-15 is now UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. The other records rest on their parts' re-reading of the code and have not had a second-helper check; no whole-AUDIT second check is claimed.

## Appendix A — STUDY statements that the AUDIT refines

The independent check of the STUDY corrected 75 points before this AUDIT was assembled (STUDY section 1.5), among them the holiday guard's exit 2 for an unreadable or malformed holiday file, the direct call of `build_direction_map` by the EOD square-off, and the `sr_detector.max_queue` reading. The AUDIT adds or refines:

- Differing defaults: STUDY 4.4 gives 13 in `system_config.yaml` L21 to L357 and 26 in the whole file; part L counts 31 across the eight registry files (13 in L21 to L357; 18 more in L359 onward, `scoring_weights.yaml` and `scan_webhook_map.yaml`), of which 10 and 18 match their field comments (L-42, L-43) and the rest are L-04, L-06 and L-13.
- The auditor: B5 is a BLOCK in a group that the guide and the pre-flight describe as WARN-only (V-05); a failing A5 probe lets the A5 boot gate pass silently (V-06); G1 measures the entry window against the 15:17 backstop, not the first MIS pass (V-09).
- Lax coercion: INTERPRETATION (pydantic, on synthetic models): a YAML `true` in a slippage-override map becomes 1.0 before the overrides' bool test runs, so that test can never fire (L-10).
- Duplicate scanner names: the "CRITICAL" check that the map's header and P17 describe cannot fire, because the parser keeps the last duplicate (L-09).
- The revert script: the re-scan it asks for after an apply can never come back clean, because its own comment contains "TEMP" (F-20).
- The scanner URLs: all 16 follow the placeholder pattern of the module 27 instruction, while a dashboard comment calls them real (T-21, F-03).
- `scoring_weights.yaml`: the signal-age threshold is 60 s in code against 90 s in P9a and in the file's comment (T-20).
- Section 13.2 item 14: the stale line references in `system_config.yaml` comments are recorded and rejected as issues, as A01 did (section 2.3).
